### A23 — Full i18n scoping pass: Hindi/Chinese/Arabic support — 2026-08-22 — investigation complete, real numbers below
Jono asked for a real scoping pass before building anything, across translation scope, font support, RTL readiness (Arabic-specific), and effort ranking. Full findings, verified against real source:

**Translation scope — bigger than "24 helpers + 4 zones," and en.json is NOT the reference file.** Union of all keys across the 6 existing language files: **742 unique keys**. `pt.json` is the real near-complete master (718/742, 96.8%) — `en.json` has only 200 keys and is missing 542 of the full set. Real per-language gaps against the pt.json master, confirmed by direct key-diff:
  | Language | Keys present | Missing vs 718-key master |
  |---|---|---|
  | es.json | 687 | 32 |
  | fr.json | 327 | **414** |
  | de.json | 327 | **414** |
  | it.json | 327 | **414** |

  FR/DE/IT (all shipped last night) are missing the identical 414 keys each — a real, substantial gap, not evenly small like ES. Root cause of why this wasn't caught earlier: 60% of the app's 1,006 `t()` calls use the `t('key') || 'hardcoded English fallback'` pattern, so a missing key silently shows baked-in English instead of an obviously-broken placeholder.

**Confirmed real bug, not a false alarm**: the creature-system screens built in the previous session (`rewards.tsx`, `world-creatures.tsx`, partially `creatures.tsx`) use hardcoded English strings for "Submit a Creature", "World Creatures", "Set as Active", etc. — zero `t()` wrapper, even though the translation keys (`submit_creature`, `world_creatures`) already exist in most language files. These are the exact screens genuinely untranslated in every shipped language right now.

**Font support**: Nunito has no Devanagari/CJK/Arabic glyphs (confirmed, Latin/Cyrillic/Greek-extended only). OS-level automatic font substitution (CoreText on iOS, system fallback on Android) means unsupported scripts render correctly today regardless — no tofu/blank boxes, just an OS-default look rather than a branded one. Recommended real fallback fonts: *Baloo Devanagari 2* (Hindi), *Noto Sans SC* (Chinese — the honest practical choice, not a stylistic compromise; genuinely playful full-coverage CJK fonts don't really exist for body text), *Baloo Bhaijaan 2* with *Cairo* as a smaller-size-legibility fallback (Arabic).

**RTL readiness (Arabic) — zero infrastructure exists, large real effort**: `I18nManager` has 0 references anywhere in 68 app files. ~187 hardcoded LTR-assuming style declarations (58 `marginLeft`, 44 `marginRight`, 23 `borderLeftWidth`, ~46 `left`/`right` position props, 13 hardcoded `textAlign`). ~54 directional icons (back arrows/chevrons) that RN never auto-mirrors. Concentration worst exactly where the real content lives: parent screens 100% of files affected, teacher screens 91%. `flexDirection: 'row'` (used 384 times) would auto-mirror correctly with zero `row-reverse` landmines found — but only for the ~56% of files with no competing hardcoded left/right.

**Effort ranking**: confirmed Jono's assumption that Hindi ≈ Chinese (both translation-volume-bound, real font to bundle, zero structural RTL work — medium effort). Arabic is a materially larger, separate-category effort: same translation lift plus the full RTL pass above, starting from zero existing infrastructure, concentrated in the highest-traffic screens.

**Hindi/Chinese voice-recording tables**: could NOT be produced the way ES/FR/DE/IT were — those languages had real, pre-existing human-translated JSON files to read from; `hi.json`/`zh.json`/`ar.json` don't exist anywhere in the repo (confirmed directly). Jono is sourcing real Hindi/Chinese speakers separately; app-side technical groundwork (placeholder JSON structure, hardcoded-string cleanup, fallback fonts) proceeding in parallel — see below.

### A23 follow-up 1 — FR/DE/IT gap-fill — 2026-08-22 — ✅ DONE, machine-drafted/unverified, native review still needed
Follow-up finding: checked es.json specifically and confirmed it's in much better shape (32 missing vs the master) than fr/de/it (414 missing each, identical set). Jono chose speed over waiting for native speakers, given this affects builds already sitting in App Store/Play Store review — drafted all 414 keys myself (cross-referencing pt.json + es.json as real source material, both Romance languages giving solid grounding for translating into FR specifically, plus DE/IT), clearly flagged as machine-drafted/unverified, same disclaimer pattern as the encouragement phrases from two nights ago.

**Explicitly flagged for priority native review** (safety/help-seeking language, most consequential to get exactly right in a children's wellbeing app): `wellbeing_alert`, `share_with_wellbeing`, `request_support`, `checkin_saved_shared`, `alert_sent`/`alert_sent_desc`, `share_wellbeing`, `keep_private`/`private_message_note`, `zone_alert`, `mark_resolved`, `disclaimer_1`/`disclaimer_2`/`disclaimer_3` (the clinical/professional-referral disclaimer), `red_zone_name`/`red_zone_desc`, `strat_safe_space_name`/`_desc`, `strat_no_teaching_name`/`_desc`, `strat_reconnect_name`/`_desc`, `strat_repair_name`/`_desc`, `ask_for_cover`/`_desc` (teacher crisis-coverage request).

**Real, non-obvious build-timing finding** (confirmed against source, not assumed, per Jono's explicit ask): this project has **no OTA update capability** — `expo-updates` isn't installed, no `channel` config in `eas.json`. The frontend JSON files are compiled into the JS bundle at `eas build` time, so editing `fr.json`/`de.json`/`it.json` alone would NOT reach iOS build #20 / Android versionCode 3, already sitting in review. **But** `AppContext.tsx`'s `fetchTranslations` (`:424-441`) merges `{...backendData, ...localTrans}` — local (bundled) keys win where they exist, but for the 414 keys genuinely *absent* from the already-bundled local files, the live backend's values survive the merge. So the fix was applied in **both** places: the frontend JSON files (correctness for future builds) **and** the backend's `TRANSLATIONS` dict in `server.py` (`fr`/`de`/`it` sub-dicts, live on Railway, deployed immediately) — the second one is what actually closes the gap for the builds already in review, without needing a new build. Verified via AST-parsing the live `TRANSLATIONS` dict post-edit: fr 485 keys, de/it 476 keys each (up from 81/67/67).

### A23 follow-up 2 — creature-screen hardcoded strings — 2026-08-22 — ✅ DONE
Confirmed real, not a false alarm: `rewards.tsx`'s "Submit a Creature"/"World Creatures" buttons and `world-creatures.tsx`'s "Set as Active"/title/subtitle/"Fully evolved!"/active-hint text were all hardcoded English, zero `t()` wrapper — even though `submit_creature`/`world_creatures` already had real translations in most shipped languages. `creatures.tsx`'s header was correctly wired; its action buttons and status text weren't. Fixed all of it: reused the real existing `submit_creature`/`world_creatures`/`my_creatures` keys where they already existed (immediate real-language improvement, no fabrication), and added a small set of genuinely new keys for concepts that didn't exist before yesterday's creature-system build (`set_as_active`, `find_next_creature_subtitle`, `find_new_creature`, `fully_evolved`, `active_badge`, `creatures_collected`, `limited_edition`/`_expired`, `finish_creature_first`, `active_checkin_hint`, `stage`) — added to `en.json` only, with graceful `t('key') || 'English fallback'` per the app's existing convention, since drafting NEW translated content for these wasn't in scope of this specific fix. Also closed a small pre-existing gap: `en.json` itself was missing `my_creatures` (only had `my_creatures_title`), causing the fallback to silently trigger even in English.

### A24 — World Creatures / My Creatures real-usage fixes — 2026-08-22 — built, NOT deployed, awaiting Jono's own Expo Go verification before build #22
Live-tested on Expo Go by Jono (reflects current code directly). Found the real architecture gap: `student/select.tsx`'s "My Creatures" button already existed on each student's card, in the right place — it was just wired to the old, disconnected defaults-only `CreatureCollection` modal instead of the real unified screen. Small, surgical fix: repointed it to `/student/creatures` (setting `currentStudent` first, matching the existing card-tap pattern), and removed the now-dead modal state/import.

Also found a real regression from last night's Bronze Shield fix: `rewards.tsx` had no `ScrollView` anywhere — removing `creatureSection`'s `flex:1` (to fix the overlap) meant content could exceed screen height with the Continue button pushed off-screen, unreachable. Fixed by wrapping the scrollable content in a `ScrollView`, action buttons pinned outside/below it so the primary CTA is always reachable.

`world-creatures.tsx` fully rebuilt:
- Real bug fix: the screen never called `navigation.setOptions({headerShown:false})` — the default stack header (showing the raw route name, Jono's "studentworld-creatures") was rendering above the custom one. Now uses `TranslatedHeader` (the app's established pattern, already used elsewhere) — fixes the heading bug and adds the missing COH logo + home button in one move.
- New: each card auto-cycles through its stage1-4 images as a preview (1.4s interval), clearly labelled "👀 Preview" and never touching the real, separate progress-dot row below it — a student can't mistake the cycling animation for having actually obtained the fully-evolved creature. Preview stops once the creature is genuinely fully evolved (shows its real final stage).
- New: 2x2 grid per screen (`PAGE_SIZE = 4`), horizontal paging via a `pagingEnabled` `FlatList` of pages when a colour has more than 4 creatures — replaces the old single vertically-scrolling grid.
- New backend fields on `/creatures/eligible`: `featured_until` (real expiry, only set when a creature is currently featured — same join pattern as `/creatures/global`), and `country` — **global-scope creatures only**, gated behind the exact same real 5-contributor privacy threshold already used on the superadmin dashboard (`portal100.html`'s `MIN_THRESHOLD = 5`), computed across the full global population so the anonymity set is real, not just this one student's eligible subset.

**Explicit hold, per Jono's instruction**: build #21 stays in review untouched. Everything above is committed but genuinely not yet verified visually — Jono is testing each item live on Expo Go himself before deciding to cut build #22. Known open risk not yet resolved: card sizing for the 2x2 grid is computed from screen width only; whether 2 rows genuinely fit without vertical clipping on a short device hasn't been confirmed on a real screen.

### A24 follow-up — real double-heading root cause found, more Expo Go findings fixed — 2026-08-22
The double-heading bug persisted after the first fix attempt because the real cause was never in the component at all: `app/_layout.tsx`'s central `<Stack>` explicitly lists every student screen with its own `headerShown: false` override (`student/rewards`, `student/creatures`, `student/submit-creature` all have real entries there) — but `student/world-creatures` was never added to that list, so it silently fell back to the global default (`screenOptions`, headers shown, with a route-derived title). Fixed at the actual source: added the missing `<Stack.Screen name="student/world-creatures" options={{headerShown:false, title:'World Creatures'}} />` entry, matching its siblings exactly.

Also fixed: the reward page's redundant "Submit a Creature" + "World Creatures" two-button row (redundant now that My Creatures already surfaces both) collapsed to one "Find World Creatures" button; added a dismiss ✕ to the Bronze Shield badge (top-right, matching the app's established dismiss pattern) so a student can clear it and just see their creature; brought back a gentle idle bounce animation on World Creatures cards, echoing the old collection modal's more playful feel per Jono's explicit ask not to lose that polish in the redesign.

**Findings for items 5 & 6 — investigation only, no build, per Jono's explicit hold:**

- **Item 5 (country-anonymity threshold)**: confirmed Jono's instinct is correct — dropping the threshold to 1 contributor would eliminate the real k-anonymity protection the 5-contributor threshold exists for; a single-contributor country tag would let anyone who can otherwise narrow down "which student/school is in that country" directly identify the creator. A collapsible "creatures per country" leaderboard/graph respecting a real minimum threshold is genuinely buildable — it's the same aggregate-and-filter pattern the superadmin dashboard's existing `MIN_THRESHOLD = 5` world map already does (`portal100.html`), just needs a student-facing UI on the same real, already-proven computation. Not built — awaiting Jono's decision on threshold value and whether to proceed.
- **Item 6 (scope levels + submission-flow country/scope capture)**: confirmed only 3 real scope levels exist end to end — `classroom`, `school`, `global`. No "family" scope value exists anywhere in the codebase, not even as dead code (grepped specifically) — the "My Family" label students see is purely a UI relabeling of "classroom" for students without one, not a real 4th scope. Jono's belief was correct. Separately confirmed: **a teacher does not select a scope at submission time at all** — `submit-creature.tsx` has zero scope-related UI; `visibility_scope` is only ever set later, by an admin, at approval time (`/creatures/global-approve/{id}`, defaults to `"school"` if unspecified). **Country entry is also missing from the submission flow entirely** — `submit-creature.tsx` has zero country-related UI; the backend silently falls back to the submitting account's own stored profile `country` field (or `"PT"` if even that's missing) — never something the teacher actively enters or confirms at submission time.

### Urgent security fix — /creatures/approve and /creatures/reject had zero ownership check — 2026-08-22 — ✅ DONE & LIVE-VERIFIED
Found while investigating item 8. Confirmed real: any authenticated teacher/parent/school_admin could approve or reject ANY creature submission system-wide, not just ones at their own school — same severity class as earlier unauthenticated-endpoint findings, fixed immediately and separately from the rest of item 8's scoping. New shared `_can_approve_creature(user, submission)` check: superadmin/admin unrestricted (unchanged); teacher/school_admin scoped to their own resolved school; parent scoped to their own submissions only (`student_id == their account`) — the safest, most conservative reading given parents have no reliable school-affiliation field, pending a real product decision on whether parents should get broader same-school access (tracked as item 8(c) below).

**Real pre-existing data gap found while testing, not introduced by this fix**: `school_profiles` (the table the existing `/creatures/pending` teacher-scoping already depended on) is confirmed live to have exactly one unusable row (`school_admin_user_id: None`) — essentially empty in production. Using it as the *only* teacher-school resolution path would have silently locked out every real teacher, not just closed the security hole. Adjusted to fall back to the teacher's own `school_name` field (imperfect, but real and populated) when the "more correct" indirect lookup comes up empty.

**Live-verified against real production accounts and two temporary test submissions** (Kairos Montessori + Sunshine International School, both deleted afterward): a different-school teacher was correctly blocked (403) from a Kairos submission; the submitting parent correctly succeeded approving their own submission; a same-school teacher (Sunshine) correctly succeeded on their own school's submission; a different parent (not the submitter) was correctly blocked (403) rejecting the Kairos submission; superadmin correctly retained unrestricted approval authority. All 5 scenarios passed. Test data and sessions fully cleaned up afterward.

### Item 8 breakdown delivered, decisions made, (a)-(d) built + (e) label renamed — 2026-08-22 — ✅ DONE & LIVE-VERIFIED
Delivered the requested (a)-(e) breakdown as separate decision points (not one bundled feature). Jono's decisions: (a) build parent "my submissions" — smallest piece. (b) teacher "my submissions" = classroom-wide ("everything my classroom has submitted"), explicitly not just what the teacher personally logged. (c) fix the parent/school_admin overlap once (a) exists — parents should only ever see their own family's queue. (d) submitter-role tracking — cheap fix, go ahead. (e) rename the "Approved Creatures" card honestly to reflect what it actually shows, rather than rebuild it as school-scoped — a deliberately-built real school-scoped card is a separate future feature if wanted later.

**Built:**
- **`/creatures/pending`** (8c): parent branch split out from school_admin's — parents now see only their own family's pending submissions (`eq("student_id", user_id)`), not the full school queue. Teacher/school_admin/superadmin behaviour unchanged.
- **`/creatures/pending`** (8d): every row now annotated with `submitted_by_role` (`teacher`/`parent`/`unknown`) via a batch lookup against `users.role` keyed on `student_id` — no new column needed, since `student_id` already reliably holds the submitting account's id.
- **`/creatures/my-submissions`** (8a/8b): this endpoint already existed but was dead — the frontend's `creaturesApi.getMySubmissions()` had no caller anywhere. Rebuilt its logic and wired it in: parent gets their own submissions (any status); teacher gets everything their classroom(s) have submitted (`classroom_id` join against their own `classrooms` rows) plus a fallback for legacy rows with no `classroom_id` captured, deduplicated.
- **`CreatureManagement.tsx`** (shared parent/teacher component, used by `parent/creature-code.tsx` and `teacher/creature-code.tsx`): new "My Submissions" / "My Classroom's Submissions" card below the existing Pending Creatures moderation queue, with status badges (pending/approved/rejected) and rejection reason shown when rejected. Refreshes after an approve/reject action in the queue above it.
- **`portal100.html`** (8e): renamed the teacher/school_admin "✅ Approved Creatures" card to "🌍 Global Creature Feed", with a subtitle ("Every school's globally-available creatures — not just yours") stating plainly what it already shows. Label/copy only, per Jono's explicit instruction — no logic change, still calls the same `/creatures/global` endpoint. Not tracked in git (same as prior portal fixes) — written to `/Users/jono/Desktop/portal100-UPLOAD-2026-08-22.html` for manual cPanel upload.

**Real bug found live during verification, fixed immediately, unrelated to items a-d themselves**: `/creatures/pending`'s teacher branch never received the `school_profiles`-empty fallback that `_can_approve_creature` got during the Aug 22 security fix. Since `school_profiles` is confirmed empty in production, this endpoint's teacher branch always resolved `school` to `""` and silently returned zero pending submissions for every real teacher — the teacher-facing moderation queue has been showing nothing to review, full stop, for however long that gap existed. Fixed with the identical fallback pattern (`if not school: school = user.get("school_name")`).

**Live-verified against real accounts** (Kairos Montessori parent `user_750b8fb79312`, Sunshine International School teacher `demo_teacher_001`, second Kairos parent `user_534131df3ccb`, teacher's real classroom `demo_class_001`) using three temporary `creature_submissions` rows: parent A's `/creatures/pending` correctly showed only their own test submission, not the second parent's, with `submitted_by_role: "parent"` correctly annotated (8c + 8d). Parent A's `/creatures/my-submissions` correctly returned their own test row plus their real pre-existing "Waterman 2" submission (8a). The teacher's `/creatures/my-submissions` correctly surfaced a classroom-scoped test submission the teacher never personally made (submitted under the second parent's account, tied to `classroom_id: demo_class_001`) — confirms genuine classroom-wide visibility, not self-submitted-only (8b). After the fallback fix, the teacher's `/creatures/pending` correctly picked up the same submission too. All temporary submissions and sessions confirmed deleted afterward.

**Deferred, not folded into this build — logged as their own follow-ups per Jono's explicit instruction:**
- **Visual polish (from A24 follow-up)**: bringing the old `CreatureCollection` modal's real per-zone movement variety (sway/hop/shiver/bounce by colour, `CreatureCollection.tsx:40-72`), Bonus Items section, and side-by-side small-icon-list + large-focus layout into the unified `student/creatures.tsx` / `world-creatures.tsx` screens. Verified live: `creatures.tsx` currently has zero animation at all; `world-creatures.tsx` has one generic bounce (same for every colour), no bonus items, no side-by-side layout. Not urgent, wanted eventually per Jono.
- **Submission-flow scope + country capture (item 6 escalation)**: Jono decided this is a real gap worth fixing properly, not just a technicality — teachers/parents should be prompted to choose scope (Classroom/School/Global) and confirm/enter country at submission time, rather than scope silently defaulting at approval and country silently inheriting the submitter's profile. Gave an honest complexity read before building anything: this is its own pass, not an item-8-sized add, for four real reasons — (1) no scope-picker UI exists anywhere in the app yet; (2) scope options must be conditionally valid against the submitter's actual resolved classroom/school context, same logic as `world-creatures.tsx`'s `scope_pref` pills but not yet threaded into the submission flow; (3) a real data-format inconsistency needs resolving first — the only existing country-picker precedent (`COUNTRY_FLAGS` in `admin/dashboard.tsx`) stores full country names, while the creature system's `country` field is an ISO-2 code (falls back to `"PT"`, `server.py:7210`) — reusing that list as-is would silently break the already-shipped country-of-origin/threshold feature; (4) a genuine workflow decision, not just a form field — does the submitter's scope choice become final immediately, or a request the existing superadmin global-approve gate can still override? Not built. Awaiting Jono's go-ahead as its own scoped piece.

## A25 — the "last build before #22" round: items 1-8, 2026-08-22

Jono's framing: build everything needed so #22 is genuinely the last build for a while, not another #19→#21 situation. Explicit standing instruction reaffirmed mid-round: keep building/committing/deploying without waiting for sign-off at each step, just never cut a build without explicit permission.

### Item 1 — submission-time scope + country picker — ✅ DONE & LIVE-VERIFIED
Resolved the two things Jono asked to be resolved before building: (1) country format — new `frontend/src/constants/countries.ts`, ISO-2 codes (matches the creature system's existing `country` field convention, e.g. `"PT"` fallback at `server.py:7210`) with flags computed from the code rather than hand-listed (no transcription risk across ~195 entries) — deliberately NOT reusing `COUNTRY_FLAGS` in `admin/dashboard.tsx`, which stores full names for an unrelated feature (school profile setup). (2) workflow question — decided the submitter's scope choice becomes the submission's real initial `visibility_scope` (previously only ever set later by an admin, defaulting to `"school"`), but superadmin's existing global-approve gate keeps full override authority — same two-gate architecture, just no longer starting from a silent, uninformed default.

**Built:** `GET /creatures/submission-options` (new) — resolves valid scope options server-side from a real student's classroom/school via the same `_resolve_student_classroom_school`/`_is_authorized_for_student` helpers the eligibility feed already uses, so the picker is never guessing from partial client-side data. `POST /creatures/submit` now accepts `visibility_scope`/`country`, clamps the requested scope down to whatever the real resolved data actually supports (never trusts the client outright — re-resolves server-side even if the client sends something else). New scope+country picker step in `submit-creature.tsx` (between details and photos), with a proper `Modal` for the country search (an earlier draft used `position:absolute` inside a `ScrollView`, which wouldn't have covered the screen correctly once scrolled — caught and fixed before shipping). Portal: the "Awaiting Final Approval" queue cards now show `📋 Requested: {scope}`, so superadmin's override is an informed decision rather than a blind one (a real, small gap found while checking whether the two-gate design actually worked as intended — the approve buttons had no visibility into what was originally requested).

**Live-verified** against `demo_teacher_001` / real classroom-linked student `dstu001` (Sunshine International School): `/creatures/submission-options` correctly returns `has_classroom:false` with no student selected vs. `has_classroom:true,has_school:true` with `dstu001` selected. Three real submit calls confirmed the clamp logic exactly: global-scope request with a real student + explicit country → stored as requested (`global`/`JP`, no clamp); classroom-scope request with NO real student → correctly clamped down to `global`; classroom-scope request WITH a real classroom-linked student → correctly stored as `classroom`, no clamp. All test submissions, the test code, and the test session deleted afterward.

### Item 2 — visual polish restored — ✅ DONE (movement + detail view), ⚠️ side-by-side adapted, not ported 1:1
Ported the old `CreatureCollection.tsx` modal's real per-zone movement (sway/blue, hop/green, shiver/yellow, bounce/default — `CreatureCollection.tsx:40-72`) into a new shared `AnimatedCreatureVisual` + `useZoneMovement` hook, generalised to animate a community creature's photo, not just a default's emoji (the old modal never had to handle that case). `world-creatures.tsx`'s cards swapped their placeholder single generic bounce (an approximation built during the A24 fix pass, not the real old behaviour) for the genuine 4-pattern variety.

Bonus Items and the "grid-select → large focused view" interaction went into a new `CreatureDetailModal`, opened by tapping a card on the My Creatures screen (`student/creatures.tsx`) — this is where they actually still make sense, not `world-creatures.tsx` (a browse/discover grid whose card action is already "Set as Active", not "inspect"). Real constraint surfaced and handled honestly rather than faked: Bonus Items (moves/outfits/foods/homes) are exclusively a default-creature concept — the 4 hardcoded creatures have an emoji+accessory system; community creatures are 4 photos and categorically can't have moves/outfits/foods/homes. The section simply doesn't render for a community creature, with real data (`rewardsApi.getCollection`, already live, was just never wired into the unified screen) for defaults. `server.py`'s `get_my_creatures` enriched with `stage_emojis`/`stage_urls` per entry so the detail modal can show the full evolution row, not just the current stage.

**Live-verified**: `/students/dstu001/my-creatures` confirmed all 4 default entries now return their full 4-emoji `stage_emojis` array (e.g. blue → `['🐟','🐬','🦈','🐋']`). Community `stage_urls` verified by code review only (same `cs.get(...)` pattern as the already-tested `stage_img` field on the line above it) — no real community unlock existed on a test account to round-trip against without creating one, and the pattern is identical to already-verified code.

**Honest note on scope**: the "side-by-side" element (small icon list + one large focused view) was NOT ported as a literal permanent split-panel layout — that would have fought the deliberately-redesigned collapsible-sections grid from A21 (itself an earlier, explicit fix for 3 fragmented old surfaces). Adapted instead as tap-to-open detail, which is the same underlying interaction (pick from a grid, see one creature large) without re-fragmenting the screen. Flagging this explicitly in case Jono wants the literal docked layout back specifically.

### Item 7 — community creatures on profile-select tick indicators — ✅ DONE
Added mid-round, alongside item 2. `select.tsx`'s per-student tick-indicator row previously only ever reflected the 4 default creatures (fed by `/rewards/batch/collections`). Now also fetches each student's actively-selected community creature per colour (`/students/{id}/my-creatures`, filtered to `type==='community' && is_active`) and renders it alongside the defaults with the same tick/in-progress treatment — using the creature's real stage-image thumbnail (not a generic icon), so multiple active community creatures for the same family don't look identical.

### Item 8 (new, mid-round) — linked-student progress continuity — investigated, confirmed structurally free, no build needed
Jono's ask: confirm whether a teacher-created student profile and a parent-linked one are the same `students` row before building continuity. Traced both real linking paths: `/parent/link-child` (`server.py:3970`) and `/family/members/{id}/link-student` (`server.py:11231`) — neither ever creates a new `students` row when linking to an existing one; both just point `family_members.student_id`/`parent_links.student_id` at the teacher's original row. `AppContext.tsx:351` already resolves this correctly client-side (`m.student_id || m.id`), and `/students/{id}/active-creatures` (`server.py:7869`) does one unconditional `.eq("id", student_id)` lookup with no role branching at all — so whichever account calls it, same row, same data. Continuity is already real and structurally guaranteed by the data model; no architectural change needed. Not independently round-tripped live (the demo data has 4 different unrelated rows all named "Matilda", none a clean real linked-pair to test against) — confidence here comes from the code trace itself, which leaves no conditional path that could diverge, not from behavioural reproduction.

### Items 3, 4, 5, 6 — three of four already built in a prior (Aug 21) session; real findings, not re-built
Checked each against live source before building anything, per standing practice this session. Result: only item 3 had a genuine, real gap.

- **Item 4 (schools contact-details minimise/expand) — already fully built, both surfaces.** Portal: `toggleSASchoolDetails()` (`portal100.html:1377`) is a real, working expand/collapse, explicitly built Aug 21 to mirror the app's `SchoolsManager`. App: `admin/dashboard.tsx`'s `SchoolsManager` (`:158`, `expanded[school.name]` state) is the original the portal was matching. Nothing built.
- **Item 5 (collapse/expand UX polish) — already fully built.** The review plan's own two entries were the confusion source: line 702's "Queued for a future session" note was the *original* request, written before the fix; line 689's "✅ DONE" entry is the actual later resolution, and was never cleaned up to remove the stale queued note. Live-verified directly in `parent/family-strategies.tsx:84`: `collapsedZones` initialises as `new Set(['blue','green','yellow','red'])` — all four colour groups genuinely start collapsed. Nothing built; the stale "queued" entry above is superseded by this note.
- **Item 6 (language-availability icons in Settings) — already fully built.** `settings.tsx`'s `LANGUAGES` array already has a real `hasVoice` field (`en`/`pt`: true, `es`/`fr`/`de`/`it`: false, `:16-21`), and it's genuinely rendered — an "🔇 Audio coming soon" badge (`:574-579`) shows on exactly the languages Jono described, hidden on EN/PT. Nothing built.
- **Item 3 (superadmin portal PDF export) — partially exists; the real gap is lower-value than it looked, flagging rather than building on it.** A "Global Overview" stats PDF for superadmin already exists in BOTH surfaces — portal (`downloadSuperAdminOverviewPDF`, `:6041`) and app (`SuperAdminDashboard`'s `downloadAllSchoolsPDF`, `admin/dashboard.tsx:629`), both hitting the real `/reports/pdf/school-overview` endpoint, both built Aug 21. The genuine gap is a Schools-directory/Users-directory PDF, which doesn't exist anywhere. **Did not build it**, because of a bigger finding surfaced while checking: the Schools *management* tab (where item 4's expand/collapse and any directory PDF would pull from) is sourced from `/admin/school-profiles` → the `school_profiles` table, which is confirmed live to have exactly **1 real row** in production (`school_profiles` count queried directly). Real schools like Kairos Montessori and Sunshine International School exist and show up correctly in the Dashboard's *aggregate* stats (a separately-computed path), but have no `school_profiles` row at all, so the Schools management tab — add/edit, invite codes, contact details, and now a hypothetical PDF export — is nearly empty of real data despite the system clearly having many real schools. Building a nicer PDF export on top of that right now would just polish something structurally broken. This is a bigger, separate decision (why schools aren't landing in `school_profiles` — no onboarding flow populates it, or a backfill is needed) than an item-3-sized PDF button. Flagged for Jono to prioritize, not silently built around.

### Deploy status
All of items 1, 2, 7 committed, pushed, and confirmed deployed (Railway `Application startup complete` after each push). No new iOS/Android build cut — per Jono's standing instruction, #22 waits for his own Expo Go verification of this whole round.

### school_profiles root-cause fix — 2026-08-22 — ✅ DONE & LIVE-VERIFIED
Follow-up to the item 3 investigation above. Root cause was singular and precise: `POST /admin/create-school-admin` (`server.py:10378`) is the ONLY real path that creates a school_admin account, and it never created a matching `school_profiles` row — confirmed live, 0 of the 3 real school_admin accounts (Kairos Montessori ×2, Sunshine International School ×1, all created 2026-07-28) had one, and the 1 orphaned row that existed ("St Lucy's") had `school_admin_user_id: None`, unlinked to any real account.

**Built:** new `_ensure_school_profile(school_admin_user_id, school_name)` helper — idempotent, creates a linked `school_profiles` row with the same default shape as the portal's own "+ Add School" (`subscription_package: "starter"`, `status: "trial"`, 5 seats). Called from both branches of `create_school_admin` (new account and re-promotion of an existing user). Every school_admin account created from now on gets a real, linked profile automatically.

**Backfill run** for the 3 real existing accounts — all 3 now have a correctly-linked `school_profiles` row. Orphaned "St Lucy's" row deleted after confirming it was still the same unlinked row (`school_admin_user_id: None`) before deletion.

**Live-verified end to end**: (1) called the real deployed `/admin/create-school-admin` with a temporary test account — confirmed a correctly-linked profile was auto-created with the right defaults, then deleted the test account/profile/session. (2) Called `/admin/school-profiles` — the exact endpoint the portal's Schools tab uses — via a temporary superadmin session: returns exactly 3 rows, all `school_admin_user_id` linked (`True`), matching Kairos Montessori ×2 and Sunshine International School. Session deleted afterward.

**Map (piece 3) — confirmed self-heals for the schools list, does NOT need a code change, and does NOT yet show pins.** `renderSAWorldMap` (`portal100.html:1144`) already handles missing coordinates honestly — it filters to `withCoords` and shows "Add latitude/longitude to a school's profile to see it appear here" rather than looking broken. The Schools tab itself (list, names, contact fields, expand/collapse, invite codes) is now fully populated and correct. The map's pins specifically still need real lat/lng entered per school via the already-working Edit form — nobody has ever entered that data, and none was fabricated here. Not a bug; a genuine remaining manual step, called out explicitly rather than left implicit.

Not yet uploaded to cPanel — no portal.html changes were needed for this fix (backend + data only), so the currently-live portal file already reflects the correct behaviour once the data existed.

Item 3 (schools directory PDF) is now real, small, and worth building whenever wanted — the same rich `school_profiles` schema (address/contact/subscription/counts) is genuinely populated for real accounts for the first time.

## A26 — live-testing round 3, 2026-08-23

### Item 1 — parent scope options — ✅ DONE & LIVE-VERIFIED — real bug, confirmed before building
Jono's report: a parent submitting only ever saw "Everyone Everywhere" as an option. Investigated before building, as asked. Root cause, precise: `get_eligible_creatures`'s "classroom" scope match (`server.py:7582`) required `classroom_id` truthy on BOTH sides (`elif scope == "classroom" and classroom_id and c.get("classroom_id") == classroom_id`). For a home-only family member (no classroom at all — the common parent case), the browsing side's `classroom_id` is always `None`, so the whole condition short-circuits false unconditionally. A "classroom"-scoped submission from a home-only family was eligible to **nobody, not even the family that submitted it** — not a picker-display issue, a genuinely non-functional scope end to end. This is why `/creatures/submit`'s own clamping logic (built in item 1's first pass) silently downgraded "classroom" to "school"/"global" whenever there was no real classroom — it was correctly protecting against creating an invisible, orphaned creature, which just meant the option was never offered at all.

Separately, for a school-linked child (where "classroom"/"school" scope IS technically functional), the "Who made this?" step that resolves which real student to check was optional and easy to skip — skipping it meant `submission-options` never got a `real_student_id` to resolve against, so the real options silently never appeared even when they should have.

**Built:** `get_eligible_creatures` now also matches "classroom"-scope submissions with no `classroom_id` against the submitting account itself (`c.get("student_id") == user["user_id"]`) — family-private, deliberately narrow to the one account that submitted it (not every account linked to that child — a home-only child only ever belongs to one parent account in the current data model, so this is the correct boundary, not a shortcut). `/creatures/submit` no longer downgrades "classroom" — it's always valid now, either as a real classroom match or a family-private one. `submit-creature.tsx`'s picker always offers the narrowest option, relabeled "My Family" when there's no real classroom (same contextual relabeling pattern `world-creatures.tsx`'s scope-preference picker already used). "Who made this?" now auto-selects when there's exactly one child, closing the easy-to-skip gap for the common case.

**Live-verified**: submitted and approved a real test creature as `user_750b8fb79312` (Kairos Montessori parent) for a real home-only family member (Mateus, `classroom_id: null`) with `visibility_scope: "classroom"`. Called `/creatures/eligible?student_id=Mateus` as the same parent — the test creature correctly appeared, confirming the family-private match works end to end, not just in theory. Negative case (a different account never matching) verified by construction — a direct string equality check has no ambiguous path — rather than an additional live round-trip, given time budget. Test submission, code, and session all deleted afterward.

### Item 2 — parent dashboard "My Creatures" navigation — ✅ DONE
Same bug class as the one already fixed once on `student/select.tsx`'s card button, just missed in a second location: `parent/dashboard.tsx`'s per-family-member "My Creatures" button (on the family dashboard grid) still opened the old defaults-only `CreatureCollection` modal (`setCollectionMember`/`setShowCollection`), never migrated when the unified screen was built. Fixed with the same `setCurrentStudent` + `router.push('/student/creatures')` pattern already used by this same file's `handleMemberCheckin` (linked child → real student record; non-linked → family-member wrapper with the resolved id). Dead modal render block, its state, and the unused `CreatureCollection` import all removed. `tsc` clean on the new code (pre-existing unrelated errors elsewhere in this large file, unchanged from before).

### Item 3 — Bonus Items celebration merge — investigated, honest scope read delivered, NOT built (as asked)
Read the old `CreatureShowcase.tsx` (the actual "slideshow" component, triggered by tapping a creature card in the old `CreatureCollection` modal — not a separate achievement-unlock event, contrary to how it might be remembered) in full before answering. This is genuinely a bigger merge than "restore a component," for five concrete reasons documented for Jono, not built: (1) different triggering/interaction models — old is an 18-second auto-advancing 5-step celebration with timers, dots, and a forced close; new `CreatureDetailModal` is a static, read-at-your-own-pace panel; merging means resolving a real interaction-design conflict, not just concatenating files. (2) the old showcase is 100% default-creature-only (emoji + moves/outfits/foods/homes) with zero concept of a community creature's photo-based stages — a community creature would need an adapted, differently-structured celebration (e.g. stage-photo reveal), which is new creative work, not a port. (3) data-loading mismatch — the new modal loads Bonus Items async with a spinner; the old showcase assumed everything was already loaded synchronously before it could even open, so a clean merge needs either a pre-fetch delay before opening or a new per-step loading state the original design never had. (4) two different bounce-animation amplitude conventions (old showcase's are larger/more dramatic than the shared `AnimatedCreatureVisual` already wired into 3 screens this session) — a real, if minor, consistency decision. (5) the real functional data the new modal added (scope, expiry, evolution row) has no natural "step" in the old slideshow's structure — needs real placement decisions, not just insertion. Recommended as its own scoped follow-up once Jono decides how much of the celebratory pacing he wants to keep vs. adapt. Not built.

### Items 1, 2, 3(visual), 5, 6 — ✅ DONE

Scope check before building: `is_linked` already existed on `/students` (`server.py:2565`), just never surfaced in the teacher-facing UI - genuinely small, comparable to other fixes this session, not a bigger-than-expected item. While wiring it up, found it was also *incomplete*: it only ever checked `parent_links`, never the other real linking path (`family_members.student_id`, set by `/family/members/{id}/link-student`) - `_is_authorized_for_student` already treats both as equally real, this endpoint didn't. Fixed both: the missing check, and the missing UI (`select.tsx`'s cards now show the same small `link`-icon badge, top-right, that the Family Dashboard's cards already had).

**Live-verified**: created a temporary `family_members` row linking a real parent account to `dstu003` (Sofia Rossi) with deliberately NO `parent_links` row - before the fix this student showed `is_linked: false`; after, correctly `true`. Confirms the fix catches exactly the case it was meant to, not just in theory. Test row deleted afterward.

### Item 8 — country-count teaser — ✅ DONE, threshold unchanged
Jono confirmed he wants to keep the 5-contributor threshold and just add momentum. `/creatures/eligible` now returns `countries_joined` (reuses `safe_countries`, already computed for the existing per-creature country reveal - zero extra query, and structurally can never leak a below-threshold country since it only ever counts countries that already cleared the same real threshold). `world-creatures.tsx` shows "🌍 {n} countries have joined - keep submitting to unlock the map!" whenever the count is above zero. **Live-verified the field is present and correctly computed** (currently `0` in production - genuinely accurate, not enough global-scope creatures from any single country yet to clear the threshold; the teaser correctly stays hidden at 0 rather than showing a hollow "0 countries" message).

### Item 3 (Bonus Items celebration) — real plan delivered, per Jono's explicit ask to see the plan before any build
Jono's brief: keep the celebratory feel/pacing, adapt it properly for both creature types rather than forcing the old fixed 5-step structure onto data it wasn't built for. Plan, not yet built:

- **Trigger stays the same real interaction**: tapping a creature card opens the celebration - matches how the old showcase actually worked (per-tap, not a separate achievement-unlock event), so no new trigger model to design.
- **Default creatures keep the exact existing 5-step structure** (Meet → Move → Outfit → Food → Home) - already good, already loved, not broken, no reason to touch it.
- **Community creatures get a new, different step sequence**, not a re-skin of the default one: (1) Meet {creature} - current stage photo. (2) Evolution journey - the real stage1→4 photo progression, framed as "look how far you've come" (this is the community equivalent of an item unlock - genuinely something to celebrate that the old showcase never had data for). (3) Scope reveal - "Visible to: My Classroom!" etc., turning the new functional data into a celebratory beat instead of bolting it on as a plain info line. (4) Limited-edition/expiry reveal - only included as a step at all when `was_featured` is true, so a normal creature doesn't get a dead/empty step. (5) Completion beat - fully-evolved celebration, or an honest "keep growing" prompt if not done yet.
- **Step count becomes dynamic**, driven by an array the caller builds per creature type, rather than a hardcoded 5 - the showcase component itself becomes generic (renders whatever steps it's given), fixing the real structural mismatch rather than working around it.
- **Solves the async-loading conflict**: step 1 (Meet the creature) opens immediately from data `creatures.tsx` already has in hand - no wait. Only later default-creature steps need Bonus Items data; if that fetch hasn't resolved by the time the auto-advance timer reaches that step, that step shows a brief loading state rather than the whole modal waiting to open (the old showcase never had to handle this, since its data was always preloaded by its parent before it could ever open).
- **Animation sizing unified**: the showcase reuses the same `useZoneMovement` hook already wired into 3 screens this session, just at a larger `size`, instead of keeping its own separate, larger hardcoded bounce values - one real animation system, not two.
- **Exit/pacing controls carry over unchanged**: skip (✕), dot pagination, Next/Exit, auto-advance - all proven, all staying. Total auto-play duration scales with real step count instead of a hardcoded 18 seconds for a fixed 5 steps.
- **Decided (2026-08-23)**: after the celebration finishes (or is skipped), hand off into the existing static `CreatureDetailModal` rather than just closing - a calm place to reference the creature afterward instead of the excitement just evaporating. Confirmed by Jono, not just the leaning noted above.

Logged as its own follow-up, same tier as the earlier visual-polish item. Not urgent - explicitly deferred by Jono, app is in a strong place. Not built.

## A28 — portal testing round, urgent safety fix, 2026-08-23

### ⚠️ ACTION NEEDED — run this migration
The urgent fix below (item 1) requires a new column. Supabase REST access has no DDL - exact SQL to run in the Supabase SQL editor:

```sql
ALTER TABLE creature_submissions ADD COLUMN superadmin_approved_at timestamptz DEFAULT NULL;
```

**Until this runs**, creature browsing/starting (`/creatures/eligible`, `/creatures/start`, "My Creatures") will correctly show/allow **nothing** for any community creature - deliberate, safe-by-default behaviour while the real gate is unavailable, not a regression. Confirmed live. `/creatures/pending`/`/creatures/my-submissions`/portal moderation queues are unaffected (different, unrelated gate) and continue working normally. Once the migration runs, existing already-teacher/parent-approved submissions will need superadmin's `global_approve_creature` action (already available in the portal/app) before they reappear - nothing is auto-approved by the migration itself.

### Item 1 — URGENT safety gap — ✅ FIXED, with a real self-correction along the way
Confirmed exactly as Jono suspected: superadmin's second approval gate was never actually enforced on any student-facing read path. `get_eligible_creatures`, `start_creature`, and `get_my_creatures` all only ever checked `status=='approved'` - the teacher/parent gate. `global_approve_creature` (superadmin's action) only ever set `visibility_scope`/`is_globally_available`, which affect WHICH scope a creature shows under, not WHETHER it's visible at all. A teacher/parent-only-approved creature was immediately visible/startable by every matching student, for every scope (classroom/school/family/global), fully bypassing superadmin.

**First fix attempt (same day) was wrong, caught immediately by live verification.** Used `global_uses` as the "has superadmin approved this" signal, reasoning it was NULL until `global_approve_creature`'s approve action set it to 0. Deployed, then live-tested end to end (not just trusted the code) - and the test failed: a creature approved by teacher only still showed as eligible. Investigated immediately: `global_uses` has a **DB-level default of 0**, confirmed by checking a truly-fresh, zero-approval submission (`global_uses: 0` from the instant of insert, before any approval at all). It's a pre-existing popularity counter (incremented when a student fully evolves a creature), never something that could have worked as an approval gate - the first fix was a silent no-op, and the real hole was still open despite looking fixed. Caught and corrected the same session, before moving on to anything else.

**Real fix**: a genuine new column, `superadmin_approved_at`, set only by `global_approve_creature`'s approve action (with a try/except fallback so that endpoint doesn't hard-fail before the migration lands), NULL on every existing row and every future row until superadmin explicitly acts. Every gated surface now selects it defensively:
- `get_eligible_creatures` (browse) - fails closed (nothing eligible) if the column doesn't exist yet.
- `start_creature` (begin pursuit) - same, plus fixed its separate missing family-private eligibility branch (parity with the fix built earlier today).
- `get_my_creatures` (My Creatures/rewards display) - joined select fails as one unit if the column is missing, lands in the existing except, fails closed.
- `add_points` (defensive fallback resolution) - falls back to the default per-colour creature rather than erroring.
- `get_awaiting_global_approval` (superadmin's own queue) - this one is NOT student-facing, so it fails *open* to its old filter if the migration hasn't landed, staying usable in the interim rather than going dark for superadmin too.

**Also found and fixed in the same investigation**: `get_awaiting_global_approval`'s own filter (`is_globally_available==False`) never actually cleared once superadmin approved a non-global-scope submission - confirmed live, 2 real already-approved submissions were stuck permanently showing as "awaiting" (fixed as part of the `superadmin_approved_at` migration, with the safe fallback above for the interim).

**Live-verified end to end, twice** - once (correctly) proving the first attempt was broken, and once proving the real fix fails safely: created a real test submission, teacher-approved it only (not superadmin), confirmed via the real deployed endpoints that it does NOT appear in `/creatures/eligible`, cannot be started via `/creatures/start`, and does not appear in `/students/{id}/my-creatures` - all correctly blocked, in the fail-closed state pending the migration. `/creatures/awaiting-global-approval` confirmed still functional via its fallback (returns the 2 real stuck submissions). All test data cleaned up afterward.

### Item 2 — scope visibility for superadmin's decision — ✅ DONE
The "Requested: {scope}" label added earlier today (A26) capitalized the raw stored value, which meant "Family (private)" and real "Classroom" scope both displayed identically as "Requested: Classroom" - the exact ambiguity item 2 was flagging. Fixed: now checks `classroom_id` presence, showing "Family (private)" distinctly from "Classroom" when there's no real classroom behind it.

### Item 3 — teacher's "My Classroom's Submissions" showing a family-scoped item — investigated, confirmed NOT a leak
Directly queried the real "Voltkit" submissions in production: there are two, unrelated, same-named creatures - one genuinely family-scoped (`jono@gmail.com`, `classroom_id: null`, correctly matches only that account), one the teacher's own separate test submission (`jono+teacher@gmail.com`, real classroom_id, correctly belongs in their own queue). Called the real `/creatures/my-submissions` endpoint as the teacher directly: returns exactly 2 rows, both genuinely the teacher's own, the family-scoped one correctly absent. No backend leak. The real, valid confusion source: the queue only ever showed the *submitting account's* name ("Joana" for both), never the real child, so two same-named creatures from different accounts were indistinguishable at a glance - directly fixed by item 4.

### Item 4 — show real student + submission date — ✅ DONE
`creature_submissions.student_name` has always been the submitting account's own display name, never the real child's (same fact already documented for `/creatures/submit`). New `_annotate_real_student_names` helper resolves the real student via `real_student_id`, applied to `/creatures/pending`, `/creatures/my-submissions`, and `/creatures/awaiting-global-approval`. Frontend (`CreatureManagement.tsx`, portal's two pending-queue renderers, portal's superadmin final-approval queue) now shows "For: {real student}" + a formatted submission date instead of just the submitting account's name.

### Items 5/6 — Schools tab quick-view expand — confirmed it already existed, found and fixed why Jono never saw it
Re-confirmed `toggleSASchoolDetails` (built Aug 21) is real, live, and working - checked the actual live portal file directly, not just the local copy. The real gap: the expand arrow and panel only ever rendered when the school profile had *any* optional contact field filled in (`details.length > 0`). Every real school today (all backfilled by this morning's `school_profiles` fix) has zero contact fields filled in - nothing prompts anyone to add them yet - so the arrow was invisibly absent for exactly the schools Jono was looking at. It wasn't missing; it had nothing to expand, and gave no indication that was the reason. Fixed: the arrow and panel now always render, with an honest "No contact details added yet - tap Edit to add" empty state instead of silently disappearing. School_admin's own view: N/A, not a comparable gap - it's a single-entity settings form (one school, their own), not a list, so there's no list-item-expand pattern to compare against.

### Item 7 — "Schools Breakdown" showing nothing at any period — ✅ FIXED, real unrelated bug found
Traced the full resolution chain live (teacher lookup → classrooms → students → feeling_logs) against Sunshine International School's real data end to end. Teacher/classroom/student resolution all worked correctly. The actual break: `feeling_logs.select("feeling_colour,zone")` - confirmed live by fetching a real row that `zone` **has never been a real column** on `feeling_logs` (the actual schema is `student_id, feeling_colour, helpers_selected, comment, location, timestamp, logged_by, family_member_id`). Every single call to this query failed with a Postgres "column does not exist" error, silently swallowed by a bare `except: pass` - so Schools Breakdown showed 0 check-ins for every school, at every time period, regardless of how much real data existed, because the query never once actually succeeded. Nothing to do with time periods or Jono's testing - just a broken query. Fixed by removing the nonexistent column. **Live-verified**: Kairos Montessori now correctly shows 125 real check-ins, Sunshine International School 840, at a 90-day window.

### Item 8 — Google Maps / easier lat-lng entry — flagged, not built (explicitly low priority)
Two real options if Jono wants to revisit this: (1) Google Maps JavaScript API's click-to-place widget - most polished, but needs a Google Cloud API key + billing enabled on an account. (2) Leaflet.js + OpenStreetMap tiles - free, no API key/billing needed, a simple embeddable "click the map, get lat/lng" widget, good enough for this use case. Since the portal is a plain hosted HTML page (not sandboxed like an Artifact), either is technically straightforward to add later - recommending option 2 as the easier, cost-free path if/when this becomes worth doing. Not built - manual entry stays as-is for now, per Jono's explicit low priority.

### Migration confirmed run, column verified correct, safety fix re-verified end to end — 2026-08-23
Jono ran the migration; Supabase reported the column already existed. Verified directly rather than assuming this was fine: checked the real column definition via PostgREST's OpenAPI schema (`format: timestamp with time zone` - correct type, matches the spec exactly) and queried every row's actual value (all 5 real rows: `superadmin_approved_at` genuinely `NULL` - no garbage, no stale data, no leftover value from anything else). Column is exactly as specified.

**Re-ran the full live verification with the real column in place** - a clean positive/negative round trip, not just the earlier fail-closed half: submitted a fresh test creature, teacher-approved it only. Confirmed blocked on all 3 surfaces (`/creatures/eligible`: absent; `/creatures/start`: 404; `/students/{id}/my-creatures`: absent). Superadmin then approved it via the real `/creatures/global-approve` endpoint. Confirmed it became eligible immediately afterward (`/creatures/eligible`: present). The one remaining block on `/creatures/start` afterward was a *different*, unrelated, correct business rule ("finish your current creature before starting a new one" - the test student already had an in-progress creature for that colour) - not the safety gate, which had already let it through by that point. Test data cleaned up. The gate is confirmed genuinely working end to end, not just deployed.

**Follow-up: Jono asked for `information_schema`-level type/default confirmation specifically.** Honest limitation: this environment only has REST (PostgREST) access via the service key, no raw SQL/RPC execution - no function for that exists anywhere in this codebase (checked). Genuine `information_schema.columns` isn't reachable this way. Did the strongest verification actually available instead, and it's arguably more direct than reading a declared default would be: inserted a raw row straight into `creature_submissions` (bypassing the app layer entirely, zero mention of `superadmin_approved_at` in the insert payload) and read back its value immediately - `None`, confirmed at the database level with no application code involved at all. Combined with the type confirmation (PostgREST's OpenAPI schema is itself derived from the real Postgres catalog, not something the API could misreport) and all 5 real production rows independently checked as `NULL`, this is multi-angle, direct empirical confirmation - the column behaves exactly as specified. Test row deleted immediately after.

### Spanish voice recordings (partial rollout) — 2026-08-23 — ✅ DONE & LIVE-VERIFIED
Jono uploaded 4 real Spanish colour-name recordings directly to Supabase Storage. Checked the real bucket structure before wiring anything up: `en/` and `pt/` both hold exactly the 28 `{key}.m4a` files (`blue`, `blue_1..6`, `green`...) `VOICE_CLIP_KEYS` expects, confirming the established convention precisely. Jono's 4 Spanish files existed but at the bucket root, named `es_emociones_{colour}.m4a` (Spanish word, not the short English key) - didn't match the convention at all, in path or naming.

The system is deliberately designed so the Storage bucket itself is the single source of truth (`get_voice_clips` lists whatever's really there and returns only keys with a real file) - so the right fix was moving the files to match, not writing special-case code around a wrong path. Moved all 4 directly via the Storage API (`es_emociones_azules.m4a` → `es/blue.m4a`, etc.) - service key has that access, faster and more reliable than asking Jono to do it by hand. Verified the move: `es/` now holds exactly the 4 correct filenames, zero leftovers at the root.

**Backend change was one line**: added `"es"` to `VOICE_CLIP_LANGUAGES`. Nothing else needed - `get_voice_clips` already only returns keys with a real file present, and the frontend (`voiceClips.ts`) already no-ops silently on any missing key (`if (!url) return;`) - the exact same mechanism already proven live for `pt`'s own partial rollout, so items 2 and 3 of Jono's ask (wire up the 4 real clips; gracefully skip the other 24) both fall out of the existing design with no new code.

**Live-verified**: called the real deployed `/voice-clips?language=es` - returns exactly `{blue, green, red, yellow}`, no helper keys. Each URL independently checked - all 4 resolve to real, correctly-sized `audio/x-m4a` files (byte sizes match the original uploads exactly, confirming the move didn't corrupt anything). Confirmed `en`/`pt` unaffected (still 28 keys each). Couldn't literally tap a colour button in a running app in this environment, so this is backend-level verification - the manifest and the audio files themselves are confirmed correct and reachable, which is what the app's already-proven playback code (`playVoiceClip`) consumes.

## A29 — two more real bugs found during Jono's own portal testing of the safety fix, 2026-08-23

### Item 1 — approved creature staying in both queues — investigated, confirmed real, same gap class I'd already fixed but missed two more surfaces
Traced it before assuming anything. `/creatures/awaiting-global-approval` (the pending queue) was already correctly gated on `superadmin_approved_at` from yesterday's fix - that part was working. The actual bug: `get_global_creatures` (`/creatures/global`, powers "Approved Creatures" both on superadmin's own card and school_admin/teacher's "Global Creature Feed") only ever checked `status=="approved"` - the teacher/parent gate - never `superadmin_approved_at`. For superadmin specifically it didn't even check `is_globally_available`. Same exact gap class as the urgent safety fix, just two endpoints I didn't touch in that first pass: this one, and `feature_creature` (a teacher/school_admin could spotlight-feature a creature superadmin had never approved).

**Confirmed live before fixing, real current exposure**: 6 real submissions (Waterman, Voltkit ×2, Waterman 2, Emberwing, Bloomwing) were showing in "Approved Creatures" while still correctly sitting in "Awaiting Final Approval" too - exactly Jono's report. And "Waterman" was **actively featured** (spotlighted, active through 2026-08-31) despite never being superadmin-approved at all.

**Fixed all three surfaces**: `get_global_creatures` now gates on `superadmin_approved_at` (fails closed if the column is ever unavailable, same direction as everywhere else). `feature_creature` (write side) now requires it before a creature can be featured at all. `get_featured_creatures` (read side) filters out any already-bad `featured_creatures` rows rather than requiring a manual data cleanup - existing rows aren't deleted, they just stop being surfaced.

**Live-verified**: confirmed all 6 previously-exposed creatures are now absent from `/creatures/global`. Confirmed "Waterman" no longer appears in `/creatures/featured`. Confirmed a real teacher account attempting to feature one of the still-unapproved creatures is now correctly blocked (400).

### Item 2 — superadmin hard delete — ✅ DONE & LIVE-VERIFIED
A soft-cancel endpoint (`cancel_creature`, sets `status: "cancelled"`) already existed in the backend but was never wired into any UI - same dead-endpoint pattern found elsewhere this session. Didn't fit either of Jono's stated use cases (removing inappropriate content, cleaning up test submissions) since the row would sit in the table forever either way. Built a real `DELETE /creatures/{id}`, superadmin-only: removes the submission row, any `creature_unlocks` referencing it (a student's real progress on a creature that would otherwise point at nothing), any `featured_creatures` rows referencing it, and best-effort Storage image cleanup (never blocks the actual delete if that part fails - no prior code in this file had ever parsed a public Storage URL back into a bucket path, so this is new, lower-confidence territory kept from being able to break the part that actually matters). Wired into the portal's "Awaiting Final Approval" queue and "Approved Creatures" card, both behind a real `confirm()` since it's irreversible.

**Live-verified**: non-superadmin correctly blocked (403). Superadmin delete succeeds, confirmed the creature is genuinely gone from `/creatures/global` afterward (not just hidden), and a second delete attempt correctly 404s. `creature_unlocks`/`featured_creatures` cleanup uses the same simple `.delete().eq(...)` pattern already proven elsewhere in this file - not separately exercised in this specific test since the disposable test creature had no real unlock/featured rows to clean up, but the same well-tested mechanism.

### Quick confirmation (not investigated further, per Jono's explicit "not urgent")
Superadmin's approval buttons showing only Classroom/School/Global (no Family) for a teacher-submitted creature (Bloomwing) - confirmed intentional, not a gap. A teacher's own submission always resolves a real classroom, so "Classroom" is already the narrowest real option; "Family" only has meaning for a home-only parent submission. One real, minor nuance flagged for the record but not fixed (per Jono's ask): the 3 approval buttons are the same fixed set regardless of submission type, so a genuinely family-scoped parent submission would show a "Classroom only" button that's technically correct once clicked (falls back to family-private matching) but slightly mislabeled for that case.

### Systematic i18n button-overflow audit — real, contained fix, delegated to a fork and verified
Investigated the reported Spanish "Buscar Criaturas del Mundo" overflow systematically, not just patched the one string, per Jono's explicit ask. Real instances found: **2**, both in `rewards.tsx`'s action-button row (the reported `find_world_creatures` button, and its sibling `my_creatures` button - same structural risk, not currently overflowing but same latent shape). Root cause: `TouchableOpacity` with `flex:1` and icon+label as separate sibling Text nodes in a row, no `flexShrink`/wrap protection, zero horizontal padding on one of the two. Checked `creatures.tsx`'s similar two-button row too - structurally different (combined emoji+text in one Text node, which RN wraps safely by default) and not a confirmed bug, hardened anyway as a precaution. Checked ~8 other flex:1/fixed-width candidates across `parent/dashboard.tsx`, `teacher/dashboard.tsx`, `teacher/bulk-checkin.tsx`, `world-creatures.tsx` - none were real instances (correct content-sized padding already, or untranslated/dynamic text). Fix applied is structural (`flexShrink`+`textAlign:'center'`+padding, letting text wrap to a second line) not a shortened string, so it holds for any future language including Hindi/Chinese/Arabic. Verified clean via `tsc` (zero new errors, all pre-existing baseline). No visual/running-app verification possible in this environment - flagged honestly.

Investigated before scoping, as asked: confirmed via the real `CREATURES` catalog (`server.py:131`) that items unlock by evolution stage (`unlocks_at_stage: 1/2/3`, uniform across all 4 default creatures, 3 items per category), not points directly and not any specific helper/strategy - `POINTS_CONFIG` awards flat, generic points per event type regardless of which strategy was used, confirming "Ask for Help" has no special connection to any item. Also checked, at Jono's explicit request, whether the OLD modal (`CreatureCollection.tsx`/`CreatureShowcase.tsx`) had any real per-item animation/sound to reuse - confirmed directly via grep that neither file has any item-specific audio at all (only generic UI-feedback sounds), so this was genuinely new work, not adaptation.

**Built**, per Jono's simplified/decided scope (category-level sounds, no tap-to-select/persist, existing `playEvolutionSound()` hook point as the trigger):

- New `BonusItemCelebration` component - 4 real `Animated` sequences (not a static fade-in): burst for moves, float-on-from-above for outfits, squash-stretch bounce for foods, slow cozy fade+scale for homes - each paired with its category's sound.
- Triggered in `rewards.tsx` at the same real stage-transition event `playEvolutionSound()` already uses, deliberately not chained off `EvolutionAnimation`'s `onComplete` - found live that `EvolutionAnimation`'s own mount condition requires `rewardsData?.evolution_info`, which the default-creature `add_points` response never actually sets, so that modal likely never shows for default creatures today. - New public Storage bucket `sound-effects` created (deliberately separate from `voice-recordings`, which is spoken narration not UI effects, and deliberately self-hosted rather than extending the existing external-CDN pattern the app's other generic sounds use). Expects `moves.m4a` / `outfits.m4a` / `foods.m4a` / `homes.m4a`. Same graceful missing-file handling as everywhere else in `sounds.ts` - confirmed live the URL currently 400s (no file yet) and the existing `playSoundUrl` try/catch already handles that silently, so the celebration plays animation-only until real files land.

Confirmed the response's `newly_unlocked` contained exactly the 4 expected items (Wave Spin/moves, Captain Hat/outfits, Sea Snack/foods, Coral Cave/homes), correctly tagged by category, at the exact check-in that crossed the threshold (not before). Test data (`student_rewards` row) fully deleted afterward, confirmed clean.

## A32 — pre-release health check (not a feature round), 2026-08-23

Jono asked for an honest "does everything actually work together" check before cutting the build - full codebase, not just this week's touched files. Results below, item by item, exactly as asked.

**1. TypeScript check, whole codebase.** Not zero errors: 37 pre-existing across the whole frontend (confirmed via `git stash` diff - all 37 predate this week's work). Two were genuinely severe, not just strict-mode noise, and are now fixed:
- `teacher/checkin.tsx`: `pinUnlocked` was read inside a `useEffect` dependency array ~270 lines before its own `useState` declaration in the same component - a real temporal-dead-zone `ReferenceError` on every mount of the teacher check-in screen (JS engines throw on this, not just TypeScript). Fixed by moving the state block up before the effects that use it.
- `subscription/success.tsx`: used `t()` in 3 places without ever destructuring it from `useApp()` - a real `ReferenceError` on the payment success/error screens, which render right after a genuine Stripe payment completes. Fixed.

Also fixed, lower severity: 3 files imported `useWindowDimensions` from `'react'` instead of `'react-native'` (wrong source, but the hook was never actually called in any of them, so dormant rather than a live crash) - removed as dead imports; 2 stale type declarations (`loginWithEmail`'s interface signature, the admin tab union missing `'schools'`/`'users'`) widened to match already-correct runtime behaviour, zero behaviour change. **37 → 24 errors.** The remaining 24 were individually triaged, not just counted - confirmed benign (stale/incomplete type interfaces like `Resource` missing `topic`/`category`, or known TS false-positives like a spread-then-computed-key object literal in `parent/dashboard.tsx` that TS can't statically prove is safe but is exactly the intended toggle-one-field pattern). One exception, deliberately NOT fixed: `teacher/student-detail.tsx`'s "Pause/Resume sharing with parent" button references undefined `BACKEND_URL`/`loadData` AND calls `POST /parent-links/school-sharing`, which doesn't exist anywhere in the backend - this button is fully non-functional (fails silently, empty catch block), but fixing it for real means building a missing backend feature, not a bug fix - flagged for a real decision, not patched.

Backend: `py_compile` clean (no syntax errors). No linter was configured in this project, so installed `pyflakes` fresh to get real coverage - 117 findings, all either unused imports/locals (cosmetic, common in a large single-file backend), 2 harmless unnecessary f-string prefixes, or 42 "dictionary key repeated" warnings clustered inside a large legacy embedded Portuguese-translation dict (pre-existing, not touched by any of this week's work - later duplicate key silently wins, meaning some earlier-written translation strings are dead code, not a functional bug since a valid Portuguese string still shows either way). Flagged, not touched - real cleanup work, out of scope for a health check.

**2. Safety-critical paths - re-verified live, fresh, today, zero regression.** Confirmed via `git diff` that the only backend commit since the safety-gate work touched three isolated spots (per-school PDF filter, the new country-leaderboard endpoint, the analytics N+1 fix) - none inside the gate/scope/delete functions themselves. Then ran a full fresh end-to-end live test against production, not just a diff-based inference: created a real teacher-approved-only (not superadmin-approved) classroom-scoped test creature → confirmed NOT eligible to a real student in that classroom → confirmed a teacher gets 403 attempting to delete it → superadmin-approved it → confirmed NOW eligible to the same classroom's real student → confirmed NOT eligible to a different classroom's student (scope isolation holds) → confirmed superadmin CAN delete it → confirmed it's genuinely gone from `/creatures/global`. All 6 checks passed exactly as expected. Test data fully cleaned up.

**3. Console errors/warnings during core flows.** Delegated a fresh, skeptical pass (not just re-reading what's already known) over check-in, creature submission, and the reward page in the real app (not the portal). Findings: both flows are clean and well-guarded. One confirmed-benign type mismatch in `strategies.tsx` (a `location` field the frontend interface doesn't declare but the backend model genuinely accepts and persists - not silent data loss). One real but harmless finding: `submit-creature.tsx` has an unconditional `console.log` on every screen load (debug leftover, not an error). Creature approval has no screen in the React Native app at all to check - confirmed again this is portal-only (matches the earlier "item 8" parity finding). Bonus Items/EvolutionAnimation code from this week is correctly guarded throughout.

**4. Cold-start crash check, all 5 roles.** No iOS simulator or device is available in this environment, so this is a static-review complement to Jono's own Expo Go pass, not a substitute for it - stated plainly, not glossed over. Traced real routing (`app/index.tsx`) to confirm each role's actual landing screen, then checked hook-call order and first-render data safety on each: student → `student/select.tsx`, teacher → `teacher/dashboard.tsx`, parent → `parent/dashboard.tsx`, school_admin/superadmin → shared `admin/dashboard.tsx` (split by `isSuperAdmin`). Specifically hunted for the same temporal-dead-zone pattern just found and fixed in `teacher/checkin.tsx` (a `useEffect` dependency array referencing a not-yet-declared `useState`) - zero matches anywhere else. No conditional/looped hook calls found (the classic React crash pattern). Lists rendered before their first fetch resolves (`students`/`classrooms`/`presetAvatars`) default to `[]`, not `undefined`, so are safe to `.map()` on first render. All 5 roles: **PASS**, with the environmental caveat above.

**5. Honest overall assessment.** Confident: the safety-critical creature-approval/scope/delete work from this week is solid - re-verified fresh today, not just carried forward from memory. Confident: the two real crash bugs found this pass (`checkin.tsx`, `subscription/success.tsx`) are now fixed and were pre-existing, not introduced by this week's feature work - neither file was touched by anything built this week before this health check. Not fully confident, flagged honestly: (a) the "Pause/Resume sharing with parent" button is genuinely broken and needs a real decision, not a patch; (b) cold-start and console-error checks are as thorough as static review allows, but nothing replaces Jono's own Expo Go pass for anything timing/rendering/device-specific; (c) the 42 duplicate-key backend translation strings are a real, if low-impact, piece of accumulated debt worth a future cleanup pass; (d) two small pieces of confirmed-dead state (`teacher/dashboard.tsx`'s `pendingCreatures`, `parent/dashboard.tsx`'s `studentCreatures`) are fetched but never rendered anywhere - harmless, but worth knowing they exist in case someone later assumes they're wired up.

All fixes from this round committed and pushed; Railway deploy unaffected (backend was not touched this round, only frontend). Still holding on cutting the build until Jono has done his own verification pass.

## A33 — portal: Approved Creatures not updating live after approve, 2026-08-23

Real UX bug Jono found live-testing: approving a creature correctly removed it from "Awaiting Final Approval" instantly, but it never appeared in "Approved Creatures" until a hard page reload - the approval itself was correct (data was right after refresh), purely a missing local-UI-update bug. Root cause confirmed: `saGlobalApprove()` only ever updated `saCreatureQueue` and re-rendered the pending list - it never touched or re-fetched `saApprovedCreatures` at all, so only `loadSACreatures()` re-running from scratch (i.e. a reload) ever refreshed that side. Fixed: after a successful approve, re-fetch `/creatures/global` and re-render `saApprovedCreatures` immediately (the exact same call `loadSACreatures()` already makes on initial page load) - both lists now update live with one action, no manual reload needed. Syntax-checked via the same `node --check` extraction method used throughout. Not yet live - part of the same pending `portal100-UPLOAD-2026-08-23.html` upload as everything else this round.
