## A34 — CRITICAL: subscription/checkout revenue path, 2026-08-23

Jono reported via screenshots: no way to actually proceed to checkout after choosing a plan on either the Parent/Family or Teacher/Educator tab. Investigated as priority 1. Turned out to be two separate, real, stacked bugs on the actual revenue path, both now fixed and live-verified.

**Bug 1 - Subscribe button permanently hidden after trial/subscription expiry.** The button was always genuinely present in code - `isActive` gated it, trusting raw `subscription_status` forever with zero expiry awareness. Confirmed live: nothing in the real trial-start flow (`POST /subscription/start-trial`) ever flips `subscription_status` away from `'trial'` - the only code that ever did that (`/trial/status`) is dead, never called anywhere in the app. So any real user whose free trial lapsed without converting - exactly the people most ready to pay - could never see a Subscribe button again. Confirmed this wasn't a fresh-signup issue (new accounts start at `subscription_status: "none"` and see the button fine) - both of Jono's own long-running test accounts were independently hitting this (`jono@gmail.com`: stuck `active`, expired since 2026-05-19; `jono+teacher@gmail.com`: stuck `trial`).

While tracing this, also found `/subscription/plans` returned a raw dict with no top-level `trial_days`, though `frontend/src/utils/api.ts`'s `getPlans()` type had always expected a wrapped `{plans, trial_days}` shape - so the displayed trial length silently always fell back to a hardcoded 30, disconnected from the real 14 (`TRIAL_DURATION_DAYS`) enforced everywhere else. Fixed both: backend now returns the wrapped shape with the real number; frontend checks real expiry (trial start + real trial_days, or `subscription_expires_at` for a lapsed paid plan) instead of trusting the status string forever.

**Bug 1b (found live-testing bug 1's fix) - the fix over-corrected: valid trials also lost the button.** `isActive` needs to stay true during a genuinely valid trial so the "trial active, X days left" banner keeps showing correctly - but that same flag was also gating the Subscribe button, meaning nobody could ever voluntarily convert early during a still-valid trial, only after it lapsed. Split into two: `isActive` (banner, unchanged behaviour) vs new `canSubscribe` (button - false only when the user has a real, currently-paying, unexpired plan; a trial, valid or expired, can always subscribe). Live-simulated against both real accounts' actual data to confirm: expired-active account -> banner hidden, button shown; valid-trial account -> banner shown ("10 days remaining"), button ALSO shown.

**Bug 2 (found closing the loop on "does clicking Subscribe actually work") - total checkout outage, both plans, unrelated to bug 1.** `POST /subscription/checkout` was 500ing for every real attempt. Root cause: `STRIPE_PRICE_TEACHER_MONTHLY` and `STRIPE_PRICE_PARENT_MONTHLY` (Railway env vars, not in the repo) both held **test-mode** Stripe price IDs while the live secret key was active - "a similar object exists in test mode, but a live mode key was used." This meant the self-serve checkout flow could never have completed a single real subscription, independent of and in addition to bug 1. Did a **read-only** listing of the real live-mode Stripe prices (no creation, no charge) and found two unlabeled live recurring-monthly prices whose amounts matched the app's displayed pricing to the cent - €7.99 and €4.99. Confirmed with Jono before touching production payment config, then updated both Railway env vars to the real live price IDs:
- `STRIPE_PRICE_TEACHER_MONTHLY` -> `price_1TmBJDGVgsNSHYytXsgnmnO8` (was a test-mode ID)
- `STRIPE_PRICE_PARENT_MONTHLY` -> `price_1TmBFSGVgsNSHYytpOIuHIMq` (was a test-mode ID)

Live-verified end to end after redeploy: both plans now return `200` with a real `cs_live_...` Stripe checkout session and a genuine `checkout.stripe.com` URL. Both test sessions immediately expired via the Stripe API afterward so no live payable links were left dangling. Honest limit stated to Jono: actually *completing* a payment requires a real human entering a real card on Stripe's own hosted page - not something I can or should simulate myself.

All frontend changes committed and pushed; Railway env var changes deployed and confirmed live. This was the actual, complete blocker on the entire subscription revenue path - both bugs needed fixing together for it to work end to end.

**Item 2 (School Package enquiry form) - built and live-verified.** The button previously opened `mailto:hello@classofhappiness.com`, which silently fails for anyone without an email app configured on their device - a real, if less visible, drop-off point right at the school-sales funnel. Replaced with an in-app Name/Email/Message form (bottom-sheet modal, matching the existing add/edit-strategy modal pattern already used elsewhere in the app) that submits directly via `fetch` to the same Formspree endpoint (form ID `mlgzvorz`) already proven on the live website's own contact form - same inbox either way, `_subject` set to "School Package Enquiry" to match the old mailto's subject line. Live-verified with a real submission matching the app's exact request shape (JSON body, `Content-Type`/`Accept: application/json`) - Formspree returned `{"ok":true}`; a real test enquiry landed in the real inbox, flagged to Jono as safe to disregard.

**Bug 3 (found live on Expo Go, AFTER build 22 was already cut and uploaded) - Subscribe crashed on native with "Cannot set location.href".** Jono hit this testing as `jono@gmail.com` (parent). Root cause: `handleSubscribe` used `typeof window !== 'undefined'` alone to decide web vs native - but Hermes/Metro can define a global `window` on native too, so this branch was wrongly taken on-device, then `window.location.href = url` threw since RN's `window.location` isn't a real navigable object. Every other web/native check in this codebase already correctly gates on `Platform.OS === 'web'` first (`auth/callback.tsx`, `AppContext.tsx` x2, confirmed via grep) - this was the one place using the bare, unreliable check alone. Fixed to match. This was the actual last step of the checkout flow - session creation was already confirmed working (bug 2 above), but the redirect to the real Stripe checkout page never happened on native until this fix.

**Important: build 22 (already uploaded to App Store Connect, and the Android artifact already produced) contains this bug.** The fix above is in the repo but not in any built binary yet - a new build is needed for this to actually reach a real device. Not rebuilt yet, pending Jono's go-ahead (and ideally a bit more Expo Go testing first, since this is exactly the kind of thing that workflow exists to catch before a build goes out).

## A35 — free-tier creature caps (collection + submission), 2026-08-24

Investigated before building, per Jono's ask: confirmed via direct source reading (grepped `submit_creature`, `get_eligible_creatures`, `start_creature` for any subscription/trial/tier reference - zero hits in any of them) that community creature access was completely unlimited for free accounts, no exceptions. This was the real, confirmed baseline the new caps were built against.

**Built, per Jono's confirmed spec:**
- **Collection cap**: free-tier accounts get 1 fully-evolved community creature per emotion colour, ever, pooled per real student (not per account - confirmed decision). New `_free_tier_colour_locks()` helper, built on the existing `_creature_unlocks_for()` real-student-aware lookup, returns which colours are already "spent." Enforced in `start_creature` (blocks moving to a genuinely different creature once a colour's capped - the existing "must fully evolve current before starting a new one" sequencing rule already in that endpoint made this a clean, natural insertion point) and surfaced as a new `locked` field on `/creatures/eligible` - locked creatures still show, faded not hidden, per spec.
- **Submission cap**: 2 total creature submissions, any status - a rejection still counts as a used try, per Jono's explicit decision. Same `free_tier_limit|` pattern already used for students/classrooms/children, applied to `creature_submissions`.
- **New `_is_genuinely_free_tier()` helper**: unlike every existing `free_tier_limit` check in this file (all of which treat any non-"none"/"free" status as "not free," including a genuinely expired trial or lapsed paid plan), this one checks real expiry - a deliberate, acknowledged divergence per Jono's explicit call ("build correctly now"), not an oversight. The older checks are flagged, not silently copied. Also respects the same school-coverage bypass every other free-tier check already uses.
- **Frontend**: locked cards in World Creatures show faded with a lock icon; tapping shows an informational-only upsell with no embedded Subscribe button (a kid could be the one tapping a creature card, not the parent/teacher who'd decide to subscribe - Jono's explicit requirement). Submission-cap errors reuse the existing "Not Now / See Plans" pattern from `profiles/create.tsx` etc., since submitting is a more deliberate account-owner action than browsing.

**Live-verified end to end against production, real data, full cleanup after:**
- Submission cap: real free-tier test account - 2 submissions succeeded, 3rd correctly blocked with the `free_tier_limit` message.
- Rejected-submissions-count check: rejected both of the free account's 2 submissions, confirmed a 3rd attempt was *still* blocked - proves rejections genuinely count toward the cap, not just pending/approved.
- **Expiry fix, the core ask**: a genuinely lapsed trial account (`trial_started_at` 20 days ago, past the real 14-day `TRIAL_DURATION_DAYS`) hit the exact same 2-submission cap as a real free account - confirms the expiry-aware check works correctly for a real lapsed account. A genuinely valid trial account (started 2 days ago) submitted 4/4 successfully with zero cap applied - confirms no false positives against a real, still-valid trial.
- Collection cap: created 2 real approved+superadmin-approved blue community creatures scoped to a real classroom; started and fully-evolved the first for a real test student (`dstu001`) under a temporarily free-tier account; the second was correctly blocked (`403 free_tier_limit`) from `start_creature`; `/creatures/eligible` correctly showed the first as `locked:false` (already theirs) and the second as `locked:true` (faded, per spec). Negative control: with the same account genuinely active again, the same second-creature start attempt succeeded with zero restriction - confirms the cap only applies to real free-tier accounts, not a blanket block.
- All test users, submissions, unlocks, and temporarily-modified shared fixtures (`demo_teacher_001`, `dstu001`) fully cleaned up and confirmed restored to their exact original state afterward.

`tsc` clean (24, same pre-existing baseline, zero new errors), backend compiles clean. Committed and pushed; not yet in any built binary (same as the native-checkout-redirect fix above - both are in the repo, neither is in build 22).

## A36 — promo code (HAPPYCLASS2026) investigation + 14-vs-30-day fix, 2026-08-25

Jono asked about a promo/bypass code he remembered but had no record of from this session - investigated real source first, no changes, before touching anything.

**Findings reported:** it's a hardcoded `PROMO_CODES` dict in `backend/server.py` (two codes actually exist - HAPPYCLASS2026 and CLASSOFHAPPINESS2026, both 30-day trials), zero Stripe involvement, redeemed via a real Settings-screen code-entry field hitting `POST /subscription/redeem-trial-code`. While tracing the real redemption path, found the actual bug: that endpoint writes its 30-day expiry into `subscription_expires_at`, but every trial-status expiry check in the file (`check_subscription_active`, and last night's new `_is_genuinely_free_tier`) only ever computed expiry from `trial_started_at` + the standard 14-day `TRIAL_DURATION_DAYS`, completely ignoring `subscription_expires_at` - so a promo-code account silently lost real access after 14 of its advertised 30 days, including hitting the brand-new creature caps 16 days early. Also found a second, apparently dead endpoint (`/auth/promo-code`) writing to an unused `promo_trial_ends_at` field that nothing in the frontend ever called.

**Fixed, both items Jono asked for:**
- New shared `_trial_is_valid(user)` helper: checks `subscription_expires_at` first when present (the real promo-granted value), falls back to the standard 14-day `trial_started_at` window otherwise - a regular trial via `/subscription/start-trial` never sets `subscription_expires_at`, so this doesn't change behaviour for non-promo trials. Both `check_subscription_active` and `_is_genuinely_free_tier` now call this one shared helper instead of each duplicating the same buggy logic.
- Removed the dead `/auth/promo-code` endpoint and its `PromoCodeRequest` model entirely - confirmed via grep it was genuinely unreachable from the frontend before deleting.

**Live-verified end to end against production, real redemption, real cleanup:**
- Created a fresh free-tier test account, redeemed the real `HAPPYCLASS2026` code through the actual endpoint a real user would hit - confirmed `subscription_expires_at` set to exactly 30 days out.
- Simulated day 20 (past the old broken 14-day cutoff, within the real 30-day window): `/subscription/status` correctly returned `is_active: true`; 3 creature submissions in a row succeeded with zero cap applied - both checks correctly honoured the real 30-day window.
- Negative control, simulated day 35 (past the real 30-day window): `/subscription/status` correctly flipped to `is_active: false`; a further submission correctly hit the `free_tier_limit` cap - confirms this is a genuine, bounded fix, not a permanent bypass.
- All test data (account, sessions, submissions, submission code) fully cleaned up and confirmed gone afterward.

Not touched, flagged only: two admin/portal user-listing endpoints (`/school-admin/users`, `/admin/users`) still include the now-permanently-stale `promo_trial_ends_at` column in their SELECT list - harmless (nothing writes to it anymore, so it'll just read null), left alone since it's outside what was asked. Also flagged, not fixed: the subscription screen's frontend trial-days display (`trialDays`, from `/subscription/plans`) is a single global 14-day figure, not personalized - a promo-code user's "days remaining" banner text would still be off past day 14, even though the real backend access is now correct. Cosmetic only, doesn't gate anything.

**Confirmed deferred by Jono**: this is a minor UI polish item for a future round, not urgent - real access is what matters and that's already correct. Logged here so it doesn't get lost: the fix would mean the subscription screen reading the user's own `subscription_expires_at` (when present, for a promo-granted trial) instead of the global `trial_days` figure to compute "days remaining," mirroring the same `subscription_expires_at`-first pattern `_trial_is_valid` now uses on the backend.

`tsc`/backend compile clean, committed and pushed, live on production (backend-only change, no frontend touched this round).

## A37 — STRIPE_TEST_MODE flipped on for Jono's end-to-end payment test, 2026-08-25

Jono asked to flip `STRIPE_TEST_MODE` on to safely test the full subscription flow with a real Stripe test card before cutting build 23. Flipped it (Railway env var), which immediately surfaced a real, previously-latent bug: `STRIPE_PRICE_IDS` always read the plain (live-mode) `STRIPE_PRICE_TEACHER_MONTHLY`/`STRIPE_PRICE_PARENT_MONTHLY` env vars regardless of `STRIPE_TEST_MODE`, unlike `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`/`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` which already correctly split test/live - so checkout creation failed outright in test mode (a live-mode price ID against a test-mode key, which Stripe correctly rejects).

Did a **read-only** listing against the real Stripe test-mode account (no creation, no charge) and found the fix didn't need new Stripe objects at all: the two price IDs that were sitting in the plain env vars *before* yesterday's live-price fix (`price_1U5oDSGVgsNSHYytSC7Wr7jm` teacher, `price_1U5oENGVgsNSHYytxQePUM0X` parent) were never wrong - they're the genuine test-mode prices, correctly priced (€7.99/€4.99) and nicknamed, just never wired to a live/test-aware env var pair. Added two new Railway env vars (`STRIPE_PRICE_TEACHER_MONTHLY_TEST`, `STRIPE_PRICE_PARENT_MONTHLY_TEST`) holding them, and gave `STRIPE_PRICE_IDS` the same test/live split the secret key already has.

Live-verified after redeploy: real checkout session creation for both plans now returns genuine `cs_test_...` sessions with valid `checkout.stripe.com` URLs. Both test sessions expired immediately after via the Stripe API, nothing left dangling.

**Current state: `STRIPE_TEST_MODE` is ON in production right now** - confirmed safe for Jono to go through a real subscription attempt with a Stripe test card (4242 4242 4242 4242). This is a global toggle, not per-session - real customers attempting to subscribe during this window would also hit the test key. Needs to be flipped back to `false` once Jono's done testing, before cutting build 23.

## A38 — post-payment UX fixes + cancel-to-free safety investigation, 2026-08-25

Three items from Jono's live payment testing.

**1. Navigation bug - fixed.** `subscription/success.tsx`'s "Continue" button was hardcoded to `/teacher/dashboard` regardless of who actually paid - a parent subscribing landed on a screen built for teachers. Now goes to `/settings`, where the subscription status this payment just changed is the actual thing worth seeing next.

**2. Settings subscription display - fixed, confirmed no new plumbing needed.** The status text used to just be the bare plan key (`subscription_plan`, a lowercase machine value like `"parent"`/`"teacher"` set by the Stripe webhook via `_get_plan_from_price`) with no indication it was even active. Now reads "Parent Subscription Active" plus a real renewal countdown. Confirmed by reading the webhook handler directly: `subscription_expires_at` already holds the real Stripe `current_period_end` (set on every `customer.subscription.created`/`.updated` event) - no new field, no new Stripe API call needed, purely a display fix using data already being stored correctly.

**3. Cancel-to-free investigation - the critical safety question, answered with both code proof and a live test.** Grepped every `UPDATE`/`DELETE` against `creature_submissions` and `creature_unlocks` in the whole file: every single one is either real progress tracking (`_progress_community_creature`), content moderation (approve/reject/superadmin hard-delete), or the account-deletion purge path - **nothing anywhere touches either table based on subscription status changing**. The Stripe `customer.subscription.deleted` webhook handler only ever updates `users.subscription_status`/`subscription_expires_at`, confirmed by reading it directly.

Then proved it live rather than trusting the code read alone: real test account, free tier, submitted 2 creatures (hit the cap, 3rd correctly blocked) → simulated subscribing (`subscription_status: active`) → submitted a 3rd successfully, uncapped → simulated cancelling back to free (`subscription_status: none`) → confirmed the real DB count stayed at 3, not reset → attempted a 4th submission → correctly blocked again, cap still maxed at the same level from before "cancelling," not reset to 0. Also confirmed there's currently no self-serve "cancel subscription, keep account" endpoint at all today - the only existing cancellation logic lives inside the full account-deletion flow, which cancels the real Stripe subscription as a side effect of deleting the whole account, not as a standalone downgrade action.

**Verdict for Jono: safe to build.** The free-tier caps are genuine, permanent, DB-level lifetime totals with zero relationship to `subscription_status` - a subscribe → exceed-free-limits → cancel → resubscribe cycle cannot reset or bypass them by design, confirmed both by reading every code path that touches these tables and by reproducing the exact cycle live. Not built yet - this was investigation only, as asked.

Items 1/2 committed, pushed, live. Item 3 was investigation-only, no code changes.

---

### FUTURE FEATURE (ready to build, not urgent) — cancel-to-free self-serve downgrade

Risk question is fully answered (see A38 above) - safe to build whenever there's time, no rush. Logging the real scope now so it's not lost:

- **What's missing today**: no self-serve "cancel subscription, keep account + progress" path exists at all. The only cancellation logic lives inside the full account-deletion flow (cancels the real Stripe subscription as a side effect of deleting the whole account) - a genuinely different, much bigger action than what this feature needs.
- **What it needs, concretely**: a new endpoint (e.g. `POST /subscription/cancel`) that cancels the real Stripe subscription (`stripe_lib.Subscription.cancel` or `.modify(cancel_at_period_end=True)` - worth deciding whether it should downgrade immediately or let the current paid period run out first, matching standard SaaS practice) and sets `subscription_status` back to `none`/`free` without touching the account, students, creatures, or any other data. Frontend: a real "Cancel Subscription" action in Settings, probably behind a confirmation step explaining what they keep (all progress/creatures) vs. what changes (the free-tier caps apply going forward).
- **Confirmed safe**: the free-tier caps (1 fully-evolved creature per colour, 2 total submissions) are genuine, permanent, DB-level lifetime totals with zero relationship to `subscription_status` - live-verified end to end that a subscribe → exceed-limits → cancel → resubscribe cycle cannot reset or bypass them.

## A39 — i18n audit + Italian voice clips (28/28 live), 2026-08-25

**i18n audit** (requested while Jono investigated a real "Portuguese pages don't fully translate" report - no code changes, investigation only):
- Confirmed root cause of the immediate PT complaint: 6 keys added to `en.json` over the last 2 days (Bonus Items labels, country-teaser text, "Back to Settings") were never backfilled into `pt.json`/`es.json`/`fr.json`/`de.json`/`it.json` - any page using them shows English mid-page in every non-English language. Small, not yet fixed - logged here, not urgent per Jono.
- Found a bigger, older, pre-existing gap while auditing: 104 keys the app's code actually calls via `t()` are missing from `pt.json`, and 98 of those are missing from `en.json` too - meaning those specific strings were never wired into the translation system in ANY language; whatever shows today is a hardcoded JSX fallback, not a real translation. Also found `en.json` itself is surprisingly small (223 keys) versus `pt.json`/`es.json` (732) and `fr.json`/`de.json`/`it.json` (755) - the non-English files carry a lot of older bulk content English never got. Not fixed - logged for a future dedicated i18n pass, scope is bigger than a quick patch.

**Italian voice clips - full rollout, 28/28 canonical clips, live-verified.** Real investigation first (see the back-and-forth on file paths/formats in the actual session - worth noting Jono caught and corrected two of his own early mistakes, including a genuinely wrong path and a false "already converted" claim, both caught by direct verification before any upload happened, not assumption). Confirmed the real, complete voice-clip picture via live source and Storage inspection:
- **Canonical 28** (`{lang}/{key}.m4a`, `VOICE_CLIP_KEYS`) - 4 colours + 24 helpers. This is the only fully-wired, production-ready system.
- **8 "batch" phrases** (Great job, How are you feeling today, etc.) - real files already exist at the Storage bucket ROOT for en/pt (16 files, flat naming, no language folder), but only 1 of the 8 (`Great_job.m4a`/`Muito_bem.m4a`) is referenced by any endpoint (`/voice-clips/reward`). The other 7 are uploaded and genuinely orphaned in every language, not just Italian.
- **4 "longer phrases"** ("Let's choose a helper," "Choose your colour," etc.) - no existing code path in ANY language. Confirmed via full grep of every voice-clip constant/endpoint in the file.

All 28 canonical Italian files uploaded to `voice-recordings/it/{key}.m4a` - real converted `.m4a` files, verified by binary content (not extension) before upload, since an earlier version of the same files were genuine `.opus` audio mislabeled/miscommunicated as already-converted. Each filename mapped against the real `strat_{colour}_{n}` ordering in `en.json` (not guessed from filename order) so every clip lands on the correct helper. `VOICE_CLIP_LANGUAGES` updated to include `"it"`. Live-verified: `GET /voice-clips?language=it` returns exactly the expected 28 keys, zero missing, zero extra; spot-checked 4 real URLs, all returned real audio bytes with `200`.

**Logged as separate future work, not Italian-specific - affects every language equally, per Jono's explicit call:**
1. **Encouragement-phrase wiring**: 7 of the 8 batch phrases (real files, every language) have no endpoint/frontend hookup at all. Needs real backend (a proper `{lang}/{key}.m4a` structure or an extended `REWARD_VOICE_CLIP_FILES`-style map covering all 8, not just 1) and frontend wiring to actually play anywhere.
2. **Longer app-narration phrases feature**: the 4 "let's pick a helper"-style clips need a real feature built from scratch - deciding which UI moments they'd play at, then the same backend+frontend wiring. Nothing to reuse here, this is new scope.

Not uploading Italian versions of either until the underlying feature exists in some language first - uploading now wouldn't play anywhere, per Jono's own reasoning.

## A40 — CRITICAL: access-control investigation and fix (password bypass, auto-account-creation, dashboard role-gating), 2026-08-26

Jono reported someone logged into the Android APK with no password and no role selected, but could see both dashboards. Treated as the single highest-priority item of the entire engagement, ahead of everything else including i18n. Full investigation against real source, then fixed and live-verified, in this order.

**Investigation findings, all confirmed against real source, nothing assumed:**
- `POST /auth/email-login` only ever checked a password `if user.get("portal_password")` - i.e. only for accounts that had explicitly opted into Settings' "Set Password" flow. Any account that never did (the default state) could be logged into by anyone who typed that email - no verification at all.
- Unrecognized emails silently created a brand-new real account with `role: "teacher"` hardcoded - not a demo/sandbox account, a genuine row in `users`.
- `app/index.tsx` showed Teacher and Parent buttons unconditionally to every logged-in user; neither `teacher/dashboard.tsx` nor `parent/dashboard.tsx` had any role check at all. Confirmed via direct grep - zero enforcement either direction.
- **A second, more severe bug found independently while investigating, not something Jono had flagged**: `POST /auth/reset-password-request` returned the real reset token directly in its API response, and `forgot-password.tsx` auto-filled it into the reset form. "Forgot password" required nothing but knowing the target's email - a full account-takeover primitive that worked even against accounts that already had a real password, since completing the flow unconditionally overwrites `portal_password`. Confirmed there is no email-sending infrastructure anywhere in this codebase (no SMTP/SendGrid/etc.) to fix this the "right" way today.
- Real blast-radius check before deciding the fix: **46 of 47 real accounts (98%) have no password set** - 25 teachers, 17 parents, 3 school_admins. An immediate hard block on no-password login would have locked out nearly the entire real user base with no self-service recovery path, especially right after disabling the broken reset flow.

**Fixed, in agreed order:**
1. **Reset-token leak - closed immediately, no downside.** `/auth/reset-password-request` no longer returns the token; same honest response whether or not the email exists (doesn't leak which emails are real accounts either). Token generation/storage untouched so a real email-delivery flow can be wired in later without a schema change. Frontend shows the honest "contact support for now" message instead of silently advancing to a token-entry step nobody has a token for, with a manual "I already have a reset code" path for Jono to hand a verified user a code out-of-band.
2. **Force-password-on-next-login - the agreed smallest safe fix for the password bypass**, not a hard block, given the 98% blast radius. New `has_password` field (from a new `_public_user()` sanitizer - also fixes a real, separate issue: `/auth/me`, `/auth/email-login`, and `/auth/google` were all returning the raw user row to the client, including `portal_password`, a real bcrypt hash). `app/_layout.tsx` redirects any authenticated session with `has_password === false` to a new hard-gated `set-password-required.tsx` screen (no back button, `gestureEnabled: false`) on every navigation until a real password is set - closes the gap for each real account the moment they're next active, without an immediate mass lockout.
3. **Auto-account-creation on unrecognized email - removed.** `/auth/email-login` now returns a real 404 "no account found" instead of silently creating one. `/auth/google` still creates real accounts on first sign-in, deliberately left alone - Google itself verifies the email is genuinely owned, that's real identity verification, not a bypass.
4. **Dashboard role-gating - built to Jono's exact intended design.** Parent-only accounts are now locked out of `teacher/dashboard.tsx`; teacher accounts can still reach both (many real teachers are also parents). Caught and fixed a real rules-of-hooks violation while building this - the first draft put the early-return before some of the component's own hooks, which would have thrown/behaved inconsistently; moved to after every hook is declared, the only safe place for a conditional return in a function component.
5. **Found and fixed a second bug live-verifying fix #3**: the "no account found" 404 came back as a 500 instead. Root cause: the inner try/except around user lookup had a broad `except Exception` that also caught every `HTTPException` raised inside it - not just the new one, but the *pre-existing* "Incorrect password" 401, "Admin PIN required" 403, and "scheduled for deletion" 403 checks too. All of them were silently being rewrapped into 500s, real status code and message never reaching the client, this whole time. Fixed with an `except HTTPException: raise` before the generic handler.

**Live-verified against production, real accounts, real cleanup:**
- Unrecognized email → real `404`, confirmed no phantom account created in the DB afterward.
- Existing real no-password account (`sarah.mitchell@sunshine-school.com`) → `200`, `has_password: false`, confirmed `portal_password`/`reset_token` no longer present in the response at all.
- PIN-protected account with wrong PIN → confirmed real `403` with its real message now (would have been a `500` before fix #5).
- Reset-token endpoint → confirmed real response contains no `token` field of any kind; confirmed the token is still generated/stored server-side (for future real email wiring) via direct DB read, then cleared the test token from the real account.

**Not independently live-verifiable**: dashboard role-gating is a client-side redirect with no API surface to test directly - confirmed via type-checking and careful manual trace (including catching the hooks-order bug above), but needs Jono's own device pass for final visual confirmation, same limitation as every other frontend-only change this engagement.

All committed, pushed, deployed, and live on production. This was treated as fully blocking - no other work continued until this was complete.

## A41 — Silent role auto-sync removed, strategies fallback bug fixed, 2026-08-26

Follow-up from A40: investigating whether a real email/password signup flow exists (it doesn't - see A40 fix #3, auto-creation was the only path) surfaced a related, lower-severity but real design flaw - `PUT /auth/role` was being called automatically and silently on component mount in two places, meaning role was never a stable, deliberate choice.

**Found (both confirmed via grep, no other call sites exist):**
- `parent/dashboard.tsx` silently called `authApiExtended.updateRole('parent')` inside its data-load try block on every mount.
- `teacher/resources.tsx` silently called `authApiExtended.updateRole('teacher')` in a mount-time `useEffect`.
- Now that role is a real access boundary (A40's dashboard role-gating), silently re-deriving it from whichever screen was last opened is no longer just sloppy - it could silently undo a deliberate role choice just by visiting the other screen.

**Fixed:**
- Both silent calls removed outright, along with the now-unused `authApiExtended` imports in each file.
- New explicit "Account Type" section added to `settings.tsx`: shows the account's current role, with a "Switch to Teacher/Parent account" button gated behind an `Alert.alert` confirmation dialog before calling `PUT /auth/role` and refreshing state via `checkAuth()`. This is now the only way role changes.
- New translation keys (`account_type`, `switch_account_type_*`, `switch_to_teacher`/`switch_to_parent`) added with real translations to en/pt/es/fr/de/it (hi/zh remain part of the already-logged, not-yet-fixed i18n gap).

**Live-verified against production** with a temporary test account (created and deleted via direct Supabase REST calls): confirmed `GET /auth/me` reflects `role: parent` initially, confirmed a deliberate `PUT /auth/role` call still correctly flips it to `teacher` and `GET /auth/me` reflects the change immediately afterward - i.e., the backend endpoint itself is untouched and still works correctly, only the frontend's silent auto-calls were removed.

`npx tsc --noEmit` run after all changes: 24 errors, identical to the established pre-existing baseline - no new errors introduced.

Also included in this same commit: the previously-queued `strategies.tsx` fallback-strategies bug fix (removed a stale, incomplete 4-of-6-item hardcoded English fallback list; added a real retry-once-then-honest-error-state with a working "Try Again" button instead).

Committed and pushed (`3c48fc69`), no backend changes so no Railway deploy needed. This closes out tonight's urgent-tier queue.

## A42 — Real email/password signup with role picker, 2026-08-26

Item #2 from the A40/A41 follow-up: after removing auto-account-creation from `/auth/email-login` (A40) and silent role auto-sync (A41), there was genuinely no email-based way left to create a new account - Google sign-in was the only real path. Scoped and built as agreed, "next-in-queue" not emergency-tier, once A40/A41 were fully wrapped and verified.

**Investigated real source first, per instruction, rather than guessing:**
- **Teacher-school linking**: existing mechanism is `POST /school/generate-invite-code` (school admin generates a `SCH-XXXX-XXXX` code) redeemed via `POST /school/join` by an already-authenticated teacher - this already lives in Settings (`schoolInviteCode` state, `handleJoinSchool`), entered *after* the account exists, not at creation time. Confirmed via grep this is the only teacher-school link path (besides a superadmin directly creating a `school_admin` account). New teacher accounts (including every existing Google-signup teacher) already start with no `school_admin_id`/`school_name` - that's the normal, expected starting state, not a gap.
- **Parent-child linking**: existing mechanism is a per-*student* 6-character `link_code` (`POST /students/link`), generated by the teacher for one specific child, entered by the parent from a modal inside `parent/dashboard.tsx` itself (`handleLinkChild`) - confirmed via grep, this is unrelated to any class/school-wide code, and the parent typically doesn't have it until after the teacher hands it over, often after the child is already enrolled.
- **Conclusion, confirmed against source**: neither linking mechanism belongs on the signup screen - both are already real, working, post-signup steps that a fresh Google-signup account goes through identically. Adding either field to the new signup screen would have built a second, inconsistent way to do something that already works. The new signup screen intentionally stays to email + password + role only, landing the new account in exactly the same "not yet linked to anything" starting state a fresh Google sign-in produces.

**Built:**
- `POST /auth/signup` (backend): validates email format, password ≥ 8 chars, name present, role is exactly `teacher` or `parent` (no default, no admin/superadmin self-serve path); 409s on an existing email pointing the user at Sign In instead; hashes and stores the password immediately (never deferred, same standard as the A40 force-password fix); creates a real session token; returns `_public_user()` (so `has_password: true` is present from the very first response - confirmed this means the A40 force-password-required redirect does not fire right after signup, since it's keyed off exactly that field).
- `frontend/app/auth/signup.tsx` (new screen): name, email, password, confirm password, and an explicit two-button Teacher/Parent picker (no default selected - must be deliberately chosen). On success, routes straight to `teacher/dashboard` or `parent/dashboard` per the chosen role, skipping the generic role-picker landing screen since the choice was just made explicitly.
- `AppContext.tsx`: new `signupWithEmail()` - deliberately re-throws on failure (unlike `loginWithEmail`/`loginWithGoogle`, which swallow errors into a generic `Alert.alert`) so the signup screen can show the real error inline (e.g. the 409 "account already exists" message points the user at Sign In specifically).
- `auth/login.tsx`: added a "New here? Create an account" link to the new screen; fixed two lines of copy that A40 made factually wrong ("New users are created automatically" / "Password is optional" - neither has been true since A40's auto-creation removal and force-password fix). Full i18n pass on this screen remains separately logged/deferred, per last night's audit.
- Google Sign-In untouched - this is a genuine addition, not a replacement.

**Live-verified against production** (temp accounts created via the real endpoint, cleaned up after):
- Teacher signup → `201`-equivalent success, `role: teacher`, `has_password: true`, no `portal_password` in the response.
- Parent signup → same, `role: parent`.
- Duplicate email → real `409` pointing at Sign In.
- Password under 8 characters → real `400`.
- Invalid/missing role (e.g. `"admin"`) → real `400`, can't self-serve into a privileged role.
- Logging in immediately afterward with the real password just set at signup → `200`, confirms the password is genuinely usable right away, not just stored.
- Wrong password against the new account → real `401` "Incorrect password" - confirms the account isn't accidentally passwordless despite going through a different code path than `/auth/set-password`.
- `has_password: true` from the very first response confirms the A40 force-password redirect cannot awkwardly re-trigger immediately after signup.

`npx tsc --noEmit`: 24 errors, identical pre-existing baseline, none in any file touched this round.

Committed and pushed (`06ca3297`), deployed to Railway, confirmed Online before live-verifying against the real deployed endpoint (not local).

## A43 — i18n gap sweep and fix: 222+69+26+83 keys, submit-creature.tsx, login/signup, and 4 more hardcoded screens, 2026-08-26

Worked through last night's i18n audit findings in the order Jono set, verifying scope against real source at each step rather than trusting the compacted summary of last night's audit numbers.

**1. Six recently-added keys backfilled into pt/es/fr/de/it** — already done in an earlier round tonight (`back_to_settings`, `countries_joined_teaser`, `new_move_unlocked`, `new_outfit_unlocked`, `new_food_unlocked`, `new_home_unlocked`); confirmed via key-diff, no action needed.

**2. The pt/es key gap** — re-derived from real source instead of trusting the "96-key" recollection: extracted every literal `t('key')` call across `parent/dashboard.tsx`, `student/zone.tsx`, `parent/widget.tsx`, `teacher/resources.tsx`, `parent/checkin.tsx`, `parent/my-wellbeing.tsx`, `settings.tsx`, `parent/family-member-stats/[id].tsx`, `teacher/students.tsx`, diffed against `en.json`'s real key set (comments stripped) — found 222 genuinely undefined keys, meaning every one of these screens was silently falling back to hardcoded English regardless of language. Also found ~14 real key-reuse bugs along the way (same key, incompatible meanings in different screens — e.g. `stats` meaning both "Stats" and "Status", `teacher_resources` meaning both the page title and a "Reviews" header) and split each into its own key rather than picking one meaning and silently breaking the other. **Mistake caught and fixed before it shipped**: first backfill pass blindly overwrote pt.json/es.json with fresh translations for all 222 keys — a pre/post diff showed ~128 of those keys already had real, previously-authored pt/es translations from earlier work that would have been silently clobbered. Reverted and redid it as add-only (never overwrite an existing value); the real residual gap was 94 keys for pt/es, 86 for fr/de/it — 94 lines up almost exactly with Jono's own "~96-key" recollection once the real pre-existing coverage is accounted for.

**3. `submit-creature.tsx`** — confirmed via grep it was genuinely 100% hardcoded (zero `t()` calls in the whole file, including a destructured-but-unused `t`). Wired all 6 step-screens (tutorial, code, details, scope/country, photos, review) and every `Alert.alert`. 69 new keys, real translations in pt/es/fr/de/it; reused the 5 existing `creature_tip_*` keys and `creature_tutorial_title` instead of duplicating them.

**4. `strategies.tsx` fallback bug** — already fixed earlier tonight (retry-once + honest error state replacing the stale 4-of-6-item hardcoded English list). Confirmed still in place, no action needed.

**5. `auth/login.tsx`** — last night's most severe finding, the actual first screen every non-English user sees. Wired every remaining label/placeholder/button/error (previously only `pin`/`enter_pin` were real `t()` calls). Also wired `auth/signup.tsx` (built earlier tonight, same 100%-hardcoded state) in the same pass rather than leaving it as a second obvious gap. 26 new keys.

**6. Sweep for other hardcoded-JSX screens** — re-ran the heuristic (t()-call density vs. literal JSX text) across all of `app/`. Found no new, previously-undiscovered screens — the 4 worst offenders were exactly the 4 already flagged last night and not yet fixed: `kiosk/index.tsx`, `about.tsx`, `auth/forgot-password.tsx`, `subscription/index.tsx`. Fixed all four (83 new keys):
- **`subscription/index.tsx`** had a real live bug found while wiring it: it already called `t('trial_active')`/`t('trial_active_desc')` for three different status-banner states, with local English fallbacks. Both keys got real values earlier tonight for *other* screens' needs — which would have made this screen start showing a doubled checkmark and the wrong sentence entirely the moment those keys resolved to something non-empty. Split into `trial_active_banner`/`plan_active_suffix`/`access_active_banner` before it could ship broken.
- **`about.tsx`**: wired the UI chrome (headers, version/copyright) but *deliberately left* the long GDPR/COPPA disclaimer, privacy-policy, and terms-of-use paragraphs in English. That's dense compliance prose, not routine UI copy — translating it accurately is a real-stakes legal-language task that deserves a dedicated reviewed pass, not an ad hoc render in the middle of a UI i18n sweep. Flagged explicitly rather than silently machine-translating it or silently skipping the file.

**Totals**: 400 new translation keys added this round (222 + 69 + 26 + 83), real translations (not placeholders) for every one across pt/es/fr/de/it, verified programmatically after every merge that pt/es/fr/de/it have exact key-parity with `en.json` and that zero pre-existing translations were overwritten. `npx tsc --noEmit` re-run after every commit: 24 errors, unchanged pre-existing baseline throughout.

Committed in 4 stages (`d05bab80`, `59193258`, `7545599d`, `1d62abff`), each pushed immediately.

**Not done, deliberately deferred**: the long legal-prose sections of `about.tsx` (flagged above); hi/zh were not backfilled this round (they were already excluded from every prior i18n round this engagement, tracked as a separate, lower-priority gap).

**Live-verification**: attempted screenshot-based verification on the real running app (home, check-in strategies, wellbeing-support modal, creature-submission) in pt/es. Not possible in this environment - macOS 12.7.6's Playwright no longer supports Chromium here, no system Chrome installed, no iOS Simulator or Android tooling available either. Home, creature-submission, and the wellbeing modal all came back clean (every key present, real distinct pt/es text, no fallback-to-English).

Checked each key against every real call site first (no hidden key-reuse conflicts this time). Fixed and pushed (`53d2bb8a`). Full pt/es/fr/de/it parity with `en.json` reconfirmed; `npx tsc --noEmit` still 24 errors, unchanged baseline.

**Honest bottom line on live-verification**: the data-level check confirms the right text is committed and would render given a working `t()` lookup - it is not the same as a human or a real device confirming the actual rendered screen, font, RTL/wrapping, or that no other runtime path (e.g. a stale cached translation bundle) intervenes. ## A44 — Big batch: cancel-to-free, legal translation, pause/resume sharing, free-tier expiry fixes, drag-reorder investigation, Nunito font, 2026-08-26

Jono gave an 11-item batch before the next build, asked to work through in order with real investigation, live-testing where possible, and per-item reporting. Items 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 9, 11 done this round (real bugs found and fixed along the way on several); 6, 7, 8, 10 deliberately paused per Jono's own explicit checkpoint request before continuing.

**1. Cancel-to-free subscription — built, live-verified end-to-end against a real Stripe test subscription.** `POST /subscription/cancel` schedules cancellation via `cancel_at_period_end=True` (not an immediate delete) — access continues until the paid period genuinely ends. `POST /subscription/resume` undoes it. No data-migration step needed on downgrade — confirmed `_is_genuinely_free_tier()`/`check_subscription_active()` already key off real expiry, and nothing in this codebase deletes creature/collection data on downgrade (only the separate, re-authenticated account-deletion flow does). Settings UI shows "Cancels on {date}" instead of the renewal countdown once cancelled, with a Resume action.
  - **Real bug found live-testing**: the `customer.subscription.updated`/`invoice.payment_succeeded` webhook handlers wrote `subscription_expires_at` as NULL whenever the event payload didn't carry `current_period_end` at the top level (Stripe's newer API versions moved it onto each subscription item instead) — not cancel-specific, this could fire on any renewal. Fixed with a fallback to the per-item field and by never overwriting a real value with nothing.
  - Full happy-path live-tested against a real Stripe test-mode subscription (customer/payment-method/subscription created via direct Stripe API calls, `STRIPE_TEST_MODE` flipped on briefly with Jono's explicit go-ahead each time, flipped back off immediately after): cancel correctly sets `cancel_at_period_end` on the real Stripe subscription (confirmed via direct Stripe API read), `GET /subscription/status` reflects it, resume correctly reverses it, `subscription_expires_at` survives both.

**2. `about.tsx`'s GDPR/COPPA legal prose — translated with real care, not a quick pass.** All 23 legal text blocks (disclaimer, privacy policy, terms of use) wired to real keys across pt/es/fr/de/it. GDPR localized to each jurisdiction's actual regulatory acronym (RGPD for pt/es/fr/it, DSGVO for de) rather than left as the English initialism; COPPA (a US statute, no EU equivalent) kept as-is rather than inventing a translated acronym; proper nouns, trademark symbols, the privacy-policy URL, and the contact email left untouched, matching standard legal-document translation practice. No terms required flagging as low-confidence — the content beyond the regulatory acronyms is descriptive factual prose (data practices, retention periods), not dense statutory language, translated directly without paraphrasing that could shift meaning. Honest caveat stated to Jono directly: a careful, good-faith translation using established terminology, not a substitute for professional legal review if this in-app text (rather than the canonical web privacy policy it already links to) is ever treated as independently binding.

**3. Real "Pause/Resume sharing with parent" — built, replacing a non-functional UI stopgap.** The existing button called `POST /parent-links/school-sharing`, which never existed on the backend (silent 404 swallowed by an empty catch) — pressing it did nothing, ever, and `school_sharing_enabled` was hardcoded `True` everywhere it was read. Built for real: `PUT /teacher/student/{id}/toggle-school-sharing`, gated to teacher/school_admin/superadmin with real per-student authorization (a linked parent cannot toggle their own visibility - this is the school's own consent control, mirroring `home_sharing_enabled` being the parent's). Pausing gates future reads only (`get_school_checkins`, `get_all_checkins_for_linked_child`, `get_school_strategies_for_linked_child`) — nothing is deleted, resuming restores full history instantly. Fixed a real multi-parent bug in the same code: the old home-sharing toggle only ever updated the *first* `parent_links` row for a student; the new endpoint updates every linked parent. Parent-side `linked-child/[id].tsx` now shows a real "sharing paused" notice using a `sharing_disabled` field the backend had returned since this was first scaffolded but nothing ever read.
  - **Two real bugs found live-testing against the real, not-yet-migrated table**: the toggle endpoint explicitly named the new column in a `select()`, which makes PostgREST error before the migration lands (unlike `select("*")`, which degrades gracefully) — 500'd instead of the intended 404. Fixed. The actual `UPDATE` can never succeed until the migration runs (expected, not fixable from application code) — but it was surfacing as an opaque 500; now returns a clean 503 with an honest, actionable message.
  - **Requires this migration** (no DDL access from code): `ALTER TABLE parent_links ADD COLUMN school_sharing_enabled boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true;`
  - Also found, not fixed (flagged, not scope-crept): `teacher/student-detail.tsx` and `parent/linked-child/[id].tsx` have ~45 pre-existing missing `t()` keys unrelated to this change — a real i18n gap in two files outside last night's 9-file sweep. Logged for a future pass.

**4. Older `free_tier_limit` checks' expiry-blindness — fixed, same pattern as the Aug 24/25 creature-cap bugs.** Six ad hoc checks (`POST /students`, `POST /classrooms`, `POST /family/members`, `link_child`'s home-school-linking gate, `generate_pdf_report`'s student-PDF cap, `create_family_custom_strategy`) all used a blind `subscription_status in ("none","free",None)` pattern, ignoring real expiry — a lapsed trial/paid account dodged these caps forever. Two also used a bare `bool(school_admin_id)` instead of the real, already-fixed school-coverage helpers (meaning a teacher/parent linked to a *lapsed* school also dodged the cap forever). Swapped all six to `_is_genuinely_free_tier()`. Live-verified with a real regression test: a teacher with a lapsed "active" subscription now correctly gets capped at exactly 10 students (would have bypassed the cap entirely before the fix).

**5. Scope-button label nuance — already fixed, sitting un-uploaded.** Confirmed real and correct in the current local `portal100-UPLOAD-2026-08-23.html` from an earlier round; nothing to build, just needs Jono's manual cPanel upload alongside everything else already pending from that round.

**9. App-side drag-reorder for Strategies+Resources — investigated, the library conflict is fully resolved, not just resolvable.** `react-native-draggable-flatlist` v4.0.3 is already installed and compatible with the current `gesture-handler`/`reanimated` versions, and there's already a complete, working, production implementation in `admin/dashboard.tsx`. Backend support already exists too: `strategies`/`school_strategies` have `order_index`, already sorted server-side, already accept it in `PUT` bodies — ready to wire into `teacher/strategies.tsx` as-is. `resources` has `order_index` and `PUT` support but nothing sorts by it server-side yet (admin/dashboard.tsx works around this with a client-side sort — the same pattern would work for `teacher/resources.tsx`). One real gap: `custom_helpers` (a teacher's *per-student* custom strategies, a different table from the reusable strategy library) has no `order_index` column at all — would need a migration to reorder those specifically. Not built yet — investigation only, per how this item was scoped tonight.

**11. Nunito font wired into mobile — built.** Bundled the real Nunito font (Google Fonts' own repository, SIL Open Font License; only a variable-weight file ships upstream, confirmed against their repo listing — no separate static per-weight files exist to fetch). Loaded via `useFonts` in the root layout, gated behind the existing splash-screen hold. Applied as the app-wide default via `Text`/`TextInput` `defaultProps` rather than touching every screen's styles individually. Verified via a real `expo export` (not just a visual claim) — Metro bundled all 1914 modules cleanly, exported asset manifest confirms `assets/fonts/Nunito.ttf` (277 kB) is correctly included. Genuine on-device visual confirmation isn't possible in this environment (no simulator/browser available here) — recommend Jono's own Expo Go pass confirms the actual rendered look.
