**Add-profile role-gating, investigated, not built (flagged for a decision):** the classroom dropdown is already effectively teacher-only, but only as an accidental side effect of `GET /classrooms` requiring a teacher-tier role server-side (a parent's `classrooms` array is just always empty) - not a deliberate frontend check. The "Class Code" field, by contrast, has zero gating and shows to everyone, including teachers adding a student directly to their own classroom, where it's redundant. Logged as a candidate fix, not built - narrower than originally suspected (only one field actually needs gating, not both).

**Creature bug + feature (item 11), real bug found and fixed, real gap wired:**
- **Bug**: a default (original) creature rendered fully invisible - opacity 0, still pressable, correct in its own detail view - whenever a submitted creature was active for the same colour. Root cause: `student/creatures.tsx`'s `CreatureGridCard` still had the exact ring-wraps-the-whole-card bug already found and fixed in the sibling file `world-creatures.tsx` (A74, 2026-08-30) - never ported over here. `is_active` is exclusive per colour, so a default losing "active" status to a community creature dropped its wrapping ring's opacity to 0, taking the entire card with it. Fixed identically to the proven A74 pattern: ring is now a purely decorative, absolutely-positioned sibling with `pointerEvents="none"`, `ringPulse`'s initial value also corrected 0.5->1 for the same "not yet animating reads as faded" reason. Confirmed this is the same component used both for a student's own My Creatures view and a parent viewing a linked child's (Matilda's) - one fix covers both.
- **Feature**: confirmed bonus items (moves/outfits/foods/homes) are effectively colour-linked, not creature-linked - each colour maps 1:1 to exactly one default creature (`FEELING_COLOUR_MAP`), so a submitted creature occupying that colour can genuinely inherit and show the same real unlock data. Wired into `CreatureDetailModal.tsx`: community creatures now fetch and display Bonus Items too, looked up via their colour's default creature id - the exact same real data, not invented.

**Filter tabs (item 13), both parts confirmed and fixed on `world-creatures.tsx`:**
- (a) `scopeChanging` state already existed (disabling the tab buttons) but was never wired to show anything - added a 300ms-delayed loader so a fast switch never flashes, tab bar and colour-filter row stay visible the whole time, only the grid area shows the loader.
- (b) Confirmed: every tab switch re-fetched the full eligible-creatures list unconditionally, every time, with zero caching. Added a per-scope session cache (`scopeCacheRef`) - switching back to an already-visited scope is now instant with no network call at all; the preference is still persisted server-side in the background.

**Student card creature redesign (item 16), built reusing existing data, two judgment calls flagged rather than assumed:**
- Reused the `creaturesApi.getMyCreatures()` call already made for every student on this screen (for `studentActiveCommunity`) - no new network request, just also kept the full per-colour data instead of discarding everything but the active community entries.
- (a) Up to 4 minis + a "×N" total-collection chip (defaults + every submitted creature - matches Matilda's real data exactly: 4 defaults + 3 community = 7, confirmed against the same live account used for the PDF investigation above, not a guess). **Flagging as a judgment call, not assumed**: implemented the chip as always-shown once total > 0 (a running total), not only when there are more than 4 - re-check this matches intent. **Also flagging**: "0-1 creature card" as a literal total is structurally impossible in the current data model - all 4 default creatures always exist in a student's collection from day one, even at zero progress, so the real minimum total is always 4, never 0 or 1. Didn't assume which was meant.
- "Most recent first" is a real, disclosed limitation: community creatures have a genuine `started_at`/`completed_at` timestamp, default creatures have no per-creature "last touched" field in the data model at all. Sort is `is_active` first (a real, live signal), then real timestamps where they exist, defaults sinking to the end unless active - correct once any community creature exists, reduces to "active first, then colour order" for an all-defaults collection.
- (b) Milestone-only border: 4 plain border-sides (top/right/bottom/left = blue/green/yellow/red), each filled only when that colour's DEFAULT creature is fully evolved - never encodes anything else, so it reads the same at any collection size. Plain View border-colours, no new library.
- (c) Chip tint hooks for 10/25/50 (bronze/silver/gold) wired now, real placeholder colours pending real design.
- **Device-verified since** (pre-flight correction, 2026-09-06): Jono confirmed in his device pass that the defaults-only fresh-student card renders correctly. The 30+ creature-count chip mock specifically remains unseen on a real device - accepted as cosmetic edge-case risk, not blocking.
- **Logged for later, not built**: "kid chooses which 4 minis display" (Clash Royale-style curation) - a good self-expression feature for a future build, per Jono's own framing.

`tsc --noEmit`: 22 errors throughout this entire round (the 23->22 drop was A84's dead-code removal, unrelated to this batch) - identical set, zero new, re-verified after every item. Static `expo export -p web` re-run clean multiple times across the round, every touched route included.

## A87 — three decisions closing item 16, add-profile role-gating built, full student-card content audit against Matilda's real data, 2026-09-06

Closes out A86's flagged judgment calls and the add-profile question. Still nothing committed - this is the last item before Jono's single device pass.

**Decision 1 (chip threshold)**: changed from "always shown once total > 0" to `totalCount > 4` only - at exactly 4 (the real minimum every student starts at), the 4 minis already say it, a "×4" chip would be pure noise. `select.tsx`'s `renderCreatureIcons`.

**Decision 2 (fresh-student card)**: confirmed as-is, nothing built. Logic trace: an all-defaults, zero-progress student gets exactly 4 sorted entries (`getSortedCreatureEntries` returns all 4 defaults, no community rows), so the chip's `>4` gate never fires; no default is `is_complete` yet, so `getMilestoneBorderStyle` returns the gray `#E5E5E5` fallback on all 4 sides. **Device-verified since** (pre-flight correction, 2026-09-06): Jono confirmed this renders correctly during his device pass.

**Decision 3 (add-profile role-gating)**: built in `profiles/create.tsx`. Gates by the ACTIVE role, not account type - `isTeacherRole` (`user.role` is `teacher`/`school_admin`/`superadmin`) sees the classroom dropdown only, no Class Code field; anyone else sees the Class Code field only, no dropdown. Matches the Google Classroom/ClassDojo convention Jono cited. `tsc --noEmit` re-checked immediately after: still 22, no new errors.

**Student-card content audit (Jono's mid-round interruption, answered against Matilda's real live data via the same temp-session pattern as the PDF investigation - both sessions deleted immediately after use)**:

Everything that can render on a card, top to bottom, and what each shows for Matilda (`student_id=49ba8ce9...`, all 4 defaults complete, 3 active community creatures, 0 not-yet-started eligible):
1. **Linked indicator** - small green link icon, icon only, no text. Shows (she's genuinely school+home linked).
2. **Selection indicator** - checkmark icon, state-only (whichever card is currently selected), no text.
3. **Avatar** - image, no text.
4. **Student name** - "Matilda".
5. **Family badge** ("🏠 Family" pill) - conditional on `is_family_member`, a different flag than the linked indicator above; not checked against her real value this pass, low-risk (static pill, unaffected by this round's changes).
6. **Creature summary text**, exactly one of three, all data-driven:
   - "⭐ X/Y pts to complete all" - gated on `totalNeeded>0 && !allComplete`. Does NOT show for her (she IS complete).
   - "✅ All creatures complete!" - gated on `allComplete`. **Shows** for her.
   - "🌟 New creatures to evolve!" - gated on a nonzero not-yet-started count. Does NOT show (her count is 0).
7. **Milestone border** (new, item 16b) - 4 independent sides, coloured only where that colour's default is complete. For her: all 4 defaults complete -> full rainbow border, every side coloured.
8. **Icon row, up to 4 minis** (new, item 16a) - active-first sort surfaces her one active pick per colour: blue-community, green-community, yellow-default (spark_pal - also carries its own ✓ complete badge), red-community. Her 3 non-active defaults (blue/green/red) don't make the visible 4.
9. **"×N" count chip** (new, item 16a/c) - total collected across all colours = 7 (4 defaults + 3 community, confirmed via `/students/{id}/my-creatures`, not guessed). `7 > 4` -> **shows "×7"**, plain-gray tint (below the 10/25/50 bronze/silver/gold tiers).
10. **"My Creatures" button** - static label, always shown.

**Redundancy flag, exactly the kind Jono's own example warned about**: for any student who has completed all 4 defaults (Matilda included), item 6's "✅ All creatures complete!" text and item 7's new full-rainbow border said the identical thing twice, independently, at the same time - text confirmed it in words, border confirmed it in colour, with no case where one was true and the other wasn't (both derive from the same 4 defaults' `is_complete` state). **Resolved same session**: Jono's call was to drop the text, the border now carries that meaning alone - `select.tsx`'s `{allComplete && (...)}` text block removed; the `!allComplete` guard on the pts-to-complete text (kept, unchanged) and the new-to-evolve text (kept, unchanged) both still reference `allComplete` so it stays a live variable, nothing else touched. `tsc --noEmit` re-checked after: still 22, no new errors.

**Queue status: fully closed.** All of round 3 (items 1-16, the add-profile investigation, and the four follow-up decisions above) is built, typechecked (22 errors, baseline, zero new), and logged. Nothing committed - Jono is starting the single Expo Go device pass across the whole batch now.

Added a subtitle under the label carrying the exact "enter code HAPPYCLASS2026 for 30 days free" text the banner used, shown only while `!hasActiveSubscription`.
- **Colour tips** → chose **Week Overview / Emotion Graph** over Resources: both dashboards already compute the same zone-count data inside these existing collapsible sections (teacher's Emotion Graph pie chart, parent's Week Overview breakdown bars), so the tip drops in with zero new fetches and sits directly beside the chart it's actually commenting on. Resources was ruled out - it doesn't currently load any check-in/zone data, so hosting the tip there would mean a new API call for content that already lives one screen away. Bonus on the parent side: the tip's dominant colour now derives from the section's own period-filtered counts instead of the banner's old "most recent single log" signal, so it responds to the selected time filter instead of ignoring it.
- Both tips keep their existing per-colour copy, per-day rotation (`COLOUR_TIPS_TEACHER`/`COLOUR_TIPS_PARENT`, unchanged), and colour-matched card styling - only the location and the dismiss-X (removed, no longer needed once it's part of a section's content instead of an interruption) changed.

**Not touched**: the separate "upgrade nudge after 5 checkins" banner on the family dashboard (`parent/dashboard.tsx` line ~1360) - a different, usage-triggered upsell prompt, not one of the three named (alert/tip/trial).

`tsc --noEmit`: still 22 errors, identical baseline set, zero new. Static `expo export -p web` re-run clean across all routes including the four touched files. Nothing committed - pending Jono's re-run of the device pass on this cleaner build.

## A89 — build 27, logged only, not built: OS-level push notifications + trial-conversion sequence, 2026-09-06

Per Jono's explicit instruction: log the plan, do not build. Scheduled with/after the Expo SDK 54→57 upgrade, since remote push requires `expo-notifications` + a development build - it does not work in Expo Go, unlike everything built so far this session.

**Core alert push (Marisa's request)**: a new alert pushes to the linked parent's and/or teacher's device(s). Tap deep-links straight to the relevant in-app alerts screen (`/teacher/alerts` or `/parent/alerts`) - never just the dashboard, since the alert tile/badge already lives there and a dashboard-only deep link would just make the person hunt for what triggered the push. Lock-screen/notification text is deliberately generic - no student names, no emotion/colour words - consistent with how in-app alert banners never named students either.

**Trial-conversion push sequence**, research-backed per Jono's own citations (SaaS touch studies + GitLab's experiment both point the same way: 2-3 touches total beats a recurring drip, which reads as annoying with diminishing returns past that):
1. Mid-trial (~day 7 of 14): "You've [logged N check-ins / supported N students] with Class of Happiness 🎉 — [X] days left in your free trial."
2. 3 days left: "[N check-ins] and counting. Your trial ends in 3 days — subscribe to keep going."
3. Last day: "Your free trial ends today — tap to subscribe."

Each fires exactly once (needs a persisted "which touches have fired" flag per account - not built yet, just noted as a requirement). Each deep-links to the subscription/payment screen, not the dashboard. The usage stat is pulled per-account and picks whichever framing is more impressive for that user (check-ins logged vs. students supported) - both numbers already exist server-side (recentLogs/analytics counts, student roster size), nothing new to compute, just to surface at send time.

**In-app fallback**: same 3-touch sequence rendered directly on the subscription screen for anyone without push permission granted - same one-shot-per-touch persistence, same copy, same destination, so a denied-permission user isn't just silently dropped from the whole conversion nudge.

Nothing built. Revisit once the SDK upgrade branch is underway.

## A90 — build-26 device-pass crash: parent role hitting teacher-only endpoints, full call-site audit, 2026-09-06

Blocking crash found on Jono's build-26 device pass, fixed before he continued to checklist items 23-26. Two crashes reported (student-select flow, then a second one mid-report from background load) turned out to be the same root cause hit at two call sites, plus a third found during the requested audit.

**Root cause**: `GET /students` and `GET /classrooms` are both deliberately teacher/school_admin/superadmin-only server-side (403 "Teacher access required" otherwise - a real Aug 26 security fix, not a bug). Three frontend call sites called one of these unconditionally, for every role, before ever checking whether the active role could use it:
1. `AppContext.tsx`'s `refreshStudents()` called `GET /students` before its own `isParentUser` check (used later in the same function to merge family data) - a parent account's 403 aborted the function before the family-members fetch (the actually-correct source) ever ran, and Expo Go's dev build surfaces `console.error` as a full-screen LogBox overlay, which is what read as a "crash."
2. `AppContext.tsx`'s `refreshClassrooms()`, called unconditionally from the background data-loader (`initialize()`'s `authChecks`) for every authenticated session regardless of role - same 403, same LogBox surfacing, this time on every app load rather than only when entering student-select.
3. `profiles/create.tsx`'s class-join-code handler re-fetched the entire student list via the same teacher-only `GET /students` just to name-match the just-created student and get its id - for parent role (the only role that can even see the class-code field, per round 3's Decision 3) this always 403'd, silently skipping the classroom join with no user-facing error (caught by a bare `console.log`, not `console.error`, so it never LogBoxed - but the feature itself never worked for the role it targets).

**Fixes, all three call sites:**
1. `refreshStudents()`: `isParentUser` computed first; `GET /students` only called for non-parent roles. Parent/family roles skip straight to `/api/family/members` (unchanged, already correct). Catch block now also treats "Teacher access required" like the existing 401 filter (quiet, no console.error) as a second line of defense, and explicitly sets `students([])` on any failure instead of leaving stale state.
2. `refreshClassrooms()`: same `isParentUser` gate, added fresh (this function had no role awareness at all before) - parent/family roles set `classrooms([])` directly and skip the network call. Sets `classrooms([])` on any failure too.
3. `profiles/create.tsx`: removed the redundant re-fetch entirely - `studentsApi.create()` already returns the created record (`saved` from the backend), so its `id` is used directly for the classroom-join call. Zero behavior change for teacher role (which always worked), but now the join-by-code path for parent role no longer depends on a call that was guaranteed to fail - it depends only on `POST /students` succeeding, see the open finding below.

**Audited, not changed (confirmed already correct or genuinely teacher-only-reachable):** `analyticsApi`/`/analytics/classroom/all` (teacher/dashboard.tsx only), `teacherHomeDataApi`'s `/teacher/student/{id}/*` endpoints (teacher/student-detail.tsx only), `/teacher/wellbeing-sharing-status` + `/toggle-wellbeing-sharing` (settings.tsx, already gated behind `user?.role !== 'teacher'` checks), `profiles/edit.tsx`'s `studentsApi.update`/`delete` (only navigated to from teacher/students.tsx and teacher/student-detail.tsx, no parent path reaches it), kiosk generate-code/revoke (teacher/classrooms.tsx only).

**Resolved same session - Jono's call**: keep `POST /students`'s Aug 26 hardening exactly as-is, do not loosen it. Instead:
- `profiles/create.tsx`'s `handleSave` now branches on `isTeacherRole`: teacher role still calls `studentsApi.create()` unchanged; parent/family role now calls `familyApi.createMember({ name, relationship: 'child', avatar_type, avatar_preset, avatar_custom })` - the exact same server-side path (`POST /family/members`) parent/dashboard.tsx's own "Add Family Member" modal already uses, which auto-creates a real `students` row + `student_rewards` row and scopes it to the parent's own family (confirmed by reading the endpoint, not assumed - this is the real flow that created Jeffrey, item 7's test child). Parent-created profiles are now real server records, not local-only.
- The Class Join Code field/state/handler (`classJoinCode`, `joinCodeStatus`, `joinedClassName`, `handleJoinCodeChange`, and the render block) is removed entirely rather than left commented out - it was wired to a path (`POST /api/classrooms/join/{code}` against a student created via the now-bypassed `POST /students`) that can't work for the role it targets. Returns in build 27 once the new atomic join endpoint below lands.
- `tsc --noEmit`: still 22, unchanged baseline. Static `expo export -p web` clean across all routes, including `profiles/create`. Nothing committed.

**Logged for build 27, backend, own branch, not built:**
1. New class-code join endpoint: server validates the code and creates + links the student in one transaction (the code itself is the authorization) - replaces the old two-step create-then-join that never worked for parent role. Un-hide `profiles/create.tsx`'s Class Code field once this lands.
2. Audit whether any real users already have phantom device-local-only profiles from the old silent-failure fallback (parent role hitting the AsyncStorage branch, believing "Profile Created!" meant a real synced student) - and whether they need a migration/repair path (e.g., detect `local_students` entries client-side and offer to re-create them server-side via the new family path).

## A91 — build-26 device-pass fixes 2-11, all approved-with-tweaks, 2026-09-06

Ten items from Jono's continued build-26 device pass, worked while he kept testing. Two turned into real, independent bugs found along the way (item 7's pill exclusion, item 6's dead "Add more" card) rather than pure polish - called out below. Nothing committed.

1. **Inline filter label bold fix** (`teacher/dashboard.tsx`): "Today's Class Mood · 2 Weeks · Saint Antonio Room" - the whole string shared the title's `fontWeight:'700'`. Split into a nested `<Text>` so only the title stays bold; the trailing period/classroom filter indicator is now normal weight, lighter grey.

2. **Kiosk card 🎟️ resize** (`teacher/classrooms.tsx`): 22px inline glyph -> 64px inside its own 76x76 fixed square with equal padding on all sides, `justifyContent:'flex-start'` override on this one button so the icon-slot + text read as two distinct zones instead of one centered blob.

3. **Kiosk page layout** (`kiosk/index.tsx`): refresh button moved off its own row (which sat above the heading, and rendered as an empty white bar with wasted vertical space whenever no classroom was assigned) to sit directly right of the "How are you feeling / Tap your name" heading. The classroom/teacher-name row now only renders when there's actually a classroom name to show.

4. **Settings trial section state** (`settings.tsx`): the "Have a Trial Code?" subtitle only had one state (the HAPPYCLASS2026 offer text, hidden once `hasActiveSubscription`) - since that flag is also true for `subscription_status==='trial'`, a user already on a trial saw nothing there at all instead of anything reflecting their real state. Added `trialDaysLeftLabel()`, mirroring the backend's own `_trial_is_valid` fallback exactly (promo `subscription_expires_at` first, else `trial_started_at + 14 days`) so a promo trial's real duration isn't miscounted as a standard 14-day one. Shows "Trial active — X days left" while on trial, the original offer text otherwise.

5. **Family member cards, single row** (`parent/dashboard.tsx`): was splitting members into two hardcoded even/odd rows inside the horizontal scroll (a 2-row zigzag), so a newly-appended member (already correctly sorted to the end - the ordering logic was fine) could land in either row depending on parity, reading as if it hadn't actually been appended. Now one flat row. **Found & fixed in passing**: the freemium "Add more" upsell card was written *after* a `return` inside the old per-row `.map()` callback - dead code, it never rendered at all, in any build. Moved to a real sibling after the last member card.

6. **Family filter pills excluding pre-existing members** (`parent/dashboard.tsx`): root-caused, not guessed - fetched live data and found Joana's `family_members.relationship` is `"self"` (she's the parent's own wellbeing identity) while Jeffrey's is `"child"`. The pill-builder filtered to `relationship==='child'` only, so any self/partner member could never get a pill regardless of age - `getFilteredLogs()` already matches by member id generically (no child-specific logic), so the underlying filter always supported non-child members; only the pill list itself was artificially narrow. Now built from every family member, any relationship.

7. **Relocated colour-tip dismiss** (`teacher/dashboard.tsx` Emotion Graph, `parent/dashboard.tsx` Week Overview): X-dismiss + Swipeable restored on both (cheap - gesture-handler already proven safe on this exact content type, see A86/A90's Swipeable history) via a plain local `tipDismissed` state per screen - not a return of the removed page-level banner stack, just per-card dismissal now that these live inside collapsible sections.

8. **Home/school icons on check-in lists** (`teacher/dashboard.tsx`, `parent/dashboard.tsx` x2 spots): root cause was two different bugs across three render sites, all fixed to the one binary already proven correct in `linked-child/[id].tsx` (Matilda's own screen: `location==='home' || logged_by==='parent' || logged_by==='family'` -> home, else school, always show one):
   - `teacher/dashboard.tsx`'s Recent Check-ins only ever showed a conditional HOME badge with no SCHOOL counterpart at all - every school check-in (the overwhelming majority) showed no icon.
   - `parent/dashboard.tsx`'s Weekly Table View mini-icons: same HOME-only gap.
   - `parent/dashboard.tsx`'s Recent Activity list: had both badges, but as an exact match on `logged_by==='parent'`/`'student'` - missed `'teacher_bulk'` (`teacher/bulk-checkin.tsx` never sets `logged_by` to `'student'`) and `'family'`, so a teacher's own linked child checked in via classroom bulk check-in showed no icon on the teacher's *own family dashboard* view - this was the "family-dash-as-teacher" gap specifically.

9. **RefreshControl brand tinting**, all 14 screens with pull-to-refresh: `world-creatures.tsx`, `parent/{dashboard,alerts,resources,strategies,family-strategies,widget,linked-child/[id],family-member-stats/[id]}.tsx`, `teacher/{dashboard,resources,student-detail,alerts,widget}.tsx`. Native control, can't be swapped for the emoji loader - brand-tinted instead. 12 had no tint at all (native default grey/black spinner) - added `tintColor="#5C6BC0"` (the app's own indigo, same constant already used as `INDIGO` in both widget.tsx files) plus `colors={['#5C6BC0']}` (Android uses `colors`, not `tintColor` - `tintColor` alone only fixes iOS). The 2 widget.tsx files and `family-member-stats/[id].tsx` already had `tintColor` (indigo, and green respectively - kept green there as its own deliberate accent) but were missing `colors` for Android; added.

10. **Creature card height normalization** (`world-creatures.tsx`, `student/creatures.tsx`): both screens' cards had unbounded-height text blocks below a consistent-height image (badges/tags/footer variant all conditional) - a default creature (no classroom_name/country/expiry/limited-edition fields, since those are community-submission-only) sat shorter than an active or featured community creature's fuller footer. Added `minHeight` to the variable text portion on both (134 on `world-creatures.tsx`'s `cardBody`, 90 on a new wrapping View in `student/creatures.tsx`'s card), sized by actually summing the real font sizes/line-heights/margins for each screen's worst-case content combination. **Device-verified since** (pre-flight correction, 2026-09-06): Jono confirmed Aqua Buddy/Waterman card heights render correctly in his device pass.

`tsc --noEmit`: 22 errors throughout, unchanged baseline, zero new. Static `expo export -p web` clean across all routes.

## A92 — final two build-26 fixes: family-dash swipe-dismiss, creature ring colour, 2026-09-06

Last two items from Jono's device pass before commit/push/PR. Everything else from A88-A91 approved as-is.

1. **Week Overview tip swipe-dismiss** (`parent/dashboard.tsx`): X worked, swipe didn't, while the teacher dashboard's identical Swipeable (Emotion Graph tip) worked for both. Diffed the two blocks line for line - the only real difference was this one missing `width:'100%'` on the Swipeable's child View (the teacher dashboard's has it). `Swipeable` measures its child via `onLayout` to compute drag thresholds; without an explicit width the row can shrink-wrap instead of spanning the card, breaking the pan gesture's math while a plain-press X button (unaffected by that measurement) still works fine. Added, now matches exactly.

2. **Creature ring colour** (`world-creatures.tsx`, `student/creatures.tsx`): the active/selected creature's highlight ring used the flat `ACTIVE_RING_COLOUR` indigo constant for every creature regardless of colour - Spark Pal, Aqua Buddy, everything got the same blue ring, undermining the emotion-colour language the whole app is built on. Both rings now use the same `EMOTION_COLORS[emotion colour]` map already used elsewhere on each card (top border, buttons, progress dots) - Spark Pal now rings yellow, Aqua Buddy blue, etc. The small "★ Active" badge pill's background is untouched (stays indigo, a UI-chrome badge, not the "highlight border" Jono named) - flagging in case he wants that to follow suit too.

**Confirmed, not changed**: the classroom lock+copy icon stays on the teacher dashboard - the Class Code field becomes parent-facing again once build 27's join endpoint ships (A90).

`tsc --noEmit`: 22 errors, unchanged baseline, zero new. Static `expo export -p web` clean across all routes. Nothing committed - pending Jono's on-device spot-check of these two, then commit/push/PR.

## A93 — language bug batch (build 26): home-page untranslated strings fixed, PT audio mismatch root-caused, 2026-09-06

Arrived mid-session while A92's two fixes were pending spot-check. Both are now part of build 26, before commit.

**1. Untranslated home/select page strings, fixed.** `app/index.tsx` had 5 hardcoded English `<Text>` literals never wrapped in `t()` (confirmed nothing else on the page was missing it - grepped every `<Text>` on the file):
- "Loading..." and "About & Privacy" - both already had existing, fully-translated keys (`loading`, `about_privacy`) that this screen just never called. Wired in, zero new translation work.
- "Select below to begin", "Teachers Dashboard — support your students here", "Family Dashboard — support your family here" - genuinely new, no existing key. Added `select_below_to_begin`, `teacher_dashboard_subtitle`, `family_dashboard_subtitle` and hand-translated into all 10 locale files (ar/de/en/es/fr/hi/it/pt/ru/zh), matching each language's existing tone/register on this same screen. Left `Class of Happiness` (title) and `© 2026 Class of Happiness` (copyright) as literals - brand name, not translatable content, consistent with how it's treated everywhere else in the app.
- `tsc --noEmit` still 22, unchanged. All 10 JSON files re-validated as parseable.

**2. PT helper audio mismatch, root-caused - not a code bug, an uploaded-file content problem.** Traced the full path end to end rather than guessing:
- `student/strategies.tsx` fetches `GET /api/helpers?feeling_colour=blue&lang=pt`, which serves `HELPERS_PT` verbatim (`id`, `name` per card - e.g. `{"id":"b2","name":"Beber Água",...}`). Read `HELPERS_PT`'s blue array directly: order is **already correct**, matching `DEFAULT_HELPERS`' canonical English order exactly (1 Gentle Stretch, 2 Drink Water, 3 Favourite Song, 4 Cosy Spot, 5 Tell Someone, 6 Slow Breathing) - confirmed via `git log -S`, this array has never been reordered since it was first written. This rules out the exact bug class already found and fixed once before for Italian (A/Aug 30, `HELPERS_IT` - logged in this same file's history at server.py:503-514): that one really was text sitting at the wrong id position; PT's isn't.
- The card's `id` (e.g. `"b2"`) is passed unchanged (confirmed no client-side remapping - `strategy.id` flows straight from the API response through `toggleStrategy` to `playVoiceClip`) into `voiceClips.ts`'s `normalizeClipKey`, which correctly converts `b2` -> `blue_2`. That key is looked up directly in the `GET /api/voice-clips?language=pt` manifest, which maps `{clip_key: get_public_url(f"pt/{clip_key}.m4a")}` with no reindexing of any kind.
- Every step of this chain - HELPERS_PT order, id passthrough, key normalization, manifest lookup - is correct and shared by every language (ES/FR/DE/IT's own 24-helper rollouts are already logged live-verified). That leaves exactly one place left for the mismatch to live: **the actual audio file content inside `pt/blue_2.m4a`, `blue_3.m4a`, `blue_4.m4a`, `blue_5.m4a` in the `voice-recordings` Storage bucket** - i.e. the right clip was recorded but uploaded under/renamed to the wrong canonical slot.
- Supporting evidence, not just a guess: queried Storage's real file metadata directly. `blue_2.m4a` and `blue_4.m4a` share a `created_at` of `17:16:27.26x`, distinctly later than `blue_1/3/5/6.m4a`'s `17:16:26.4xx-26.5xx` cluster (all uploaded seconds apart, same batch, Aug 19) - a real, checkable sign these two were part of a separate upload pass from the other four, consistent with a content mix-up during that pass. The reported symptom's own shape supports this too: `2→3→5→2` forms a clean 3-cycle and `4→6` a 2-element pair - exactly the signature of files uploaded against the wrong slot order, not a random one-off.
- **Why not fixed outright**: confirming which specific file actually contains which spoken phrase requires listening to the audio, which this environment can't do. Per Jono's own instruction not to hand-swap blindly, this needs someone to actually listen to `pt/blue_2/3/4/5.m4a` against the canonical text (2 Drink Water, 3 Favourite Song, 4 Cosy Spot, 5 Tell Someone) and re-upload/rename whichever are actually swapped in Storage - a content fix, not a code change. Flagging blue_1 and blue_6 as unreported but worth a quick listen too, given the cycle above implies 4↔6 could be a clean swap rather than one-directional.
- **Not yet checked**: whether the same content-mislabeling pattern exists in green/yellow/red for PT, or in any other language's blue zone - only blue was reported so only blue was traced. Worth the same file-metadata check across the full 9-language x 4-colour matrix if this turns out to be a systemic upload-process issue rather than a one-time PT mistake.

**Logged for build 27, not built - correction on scope**: Jono's message described adding Arabic "as language 11" with a full audio+RTL+translation build. Checked the actual repo state first: **Arabic already exists** - `ar.json` (1379/1389 keys vs en.json, essentially complete) and is already registered in `settings.tsx`'s `LANGUAGES` list (`hasVoice:false, isBeta:true`) - it's the *current* 10th language, not a future 11th. The real remaining gaps match his own bracketed caveat exactly: no audio recordings yet (`hasVoice:false`), and (genuinely new, not yet built) **full RTL layout support** - the app has no RTL handling anywhere today (checked: no `I18nManager` usage, no `writingDirection`/directional-style audit exists), so Arabic would be the first RTL language the app has ever needed to support structurally, not just textually. Logged as: (1) RTL layout audit + implementation, (2) audio recordings once Jono confirms status, (3) a verification pass matching the same rigor this session used to root-cause the PT audio mismatch - scheduled after the audio-mapping fix above ships and is verified. (Correction to this entry's own earlier draft: it referenced an "item-4-style audit checklist" from Jono's language-batch message that this session never actually received - see A94.)

`tsc --noEmit`: 22 errors, unchanged baseline. Nothing committed.

## A95 — language-batch items 3 & 4: check-in greeting audit (inconclusive, PT provably correct) + full audio cross-reference (clean), 2026-09-06

**Item 3, check-in greeting playing in English only - investigated, could NOT reproduce or root-cause.** Traced the real path: `student/zone.tsx` calls `playPhraseFromPool('opening', language)` on mount (the `language` from `useApp()`, no hardcoding found anywhere in this path). That calls `GET /api/voice-clips/phrases?moment=opening&language=X`, which does `VOICE_PHRASE_POOLS["opening"].get(language, [])` filtered against real Storage files - no English fallback exists anywhere in this chain, checked line by line. Tested the live production endpoint directly rather than trusting the code read alone: `moment=opening&language=pt` correctly returns `Como_te_sentes_hoje.m4a`/`Regista_como_te_sentes.m4a` (the real Portuguese files, confirmed present in Storage); `language=en` correctly returns the English pair. **PT is provably correct, end to end, backend-verified live.** Two real gaps found instead, neither matching "plays English": French and Italian have **zero** entries in `VOICE_PHRASE_POOLS` for any of the 3 moments (opening/praise/farewell) - not missing files, the code never looks for language-specific clips for these two at all, so the honest behaviour today is silence, not English (Italian's gap was already known/commented in server.py; French's is the same, newly confirmed). Arabic has zero recordings of any kind (already known, `hasVoice:false`). None of this reproduces "always plays English" for any language, including PT. Flagging as unresolved - needs Jono to confirm exactly which language + screen state he saw this in, since the strongest remaining candidate (an `AppContext` `language` state race - it initializes at `'en'` and only corrects asynchronously) couldn't be confirmed or ruled out without a device.

**Item 4, full audio audit - clean, two intentional gaps only.** Cross-referenced every file the code can possibly reference against the real Storage bucket contents (queried directly, not assumed):
- **28-key zone/helper manifest** (`{language}/{colour}[_{n}].m4a`, the system `student/strategies.tsx` and `zone.tsx`'s colour taps use): en/pt/es/fr/de/it/hi/zh/ru each have **exactly 28/28**, zero missing, zero extra, filenames match `VOICE_CLIP_KEYS` precisely. `ar` has **0/28** - the entire language, not a partial gap (matches `hasVoice:false`, not new).

First play after switching took ~a minute (cold fetch), everything after played instantly - consistent with the OS/native media layer caching fetched audio on-device (independent of this app's own in-memory `phrasePoolCache`/`manifestCache`, which were already confirmed correctly keyed per-language in this same entry above and aren't the mechanism here). Conclusion: the English greeting he originally heard was a stale device-level cache hit right after a language switch, not a code bug - resolved as device cache staleness, not reproducible as a logic error, not blocking the PR.

## A96 — /students + /classrooms timeouts: diagnosed, not fixed (per explicit instruction), 2026-09-06

Jono reported persistent client-side timeouts and 429/407 errors on `/classrooms`, then refined it: `/students` and `/classrooms` specifically time out (AbortError) while creature-batch calls return 200 in the same session - failures are selective, not total. Diagnosis only, no code touched.

**1. Railway logs/metrics, last ~2h - requests ARRIVE and respond, just slowly; nothing is being rejected by Railway.**
- `railway metrics --since 6h`: CPU near-0% the entire window (max 0.02 vCPU of 8.0 available), memory 88-115MB of 8GB - the Railway container itself is nowhere near resource limits. 0% 5xx error rate. 188/1242 requests (15.2%) were 4xx - expected volume given this session's own role-gating work (403s for wrong-role calls, by design) plus normal 401s, not evidence of throttling on its own.
- Live `railway logs --http` (pulled at the time of the report): `/api/classrooms` and `/api/analytics/classroom/all` genuinely take **2-8 seconds** to respond (200 OK) - not instant, but not literally blocked either. Two real **499s** ("Client Closed Request") on `/api/classrooms` at 16:02 and 16:04 UTC - this status is Railway's proxy logging that the *client* disconnected before the server finished, consistent with the app's own `AbortController` (see #3) firing, not a server-side rejection.
- **Zero literal 429 or 407 responses anywhere in Railway's own logs**, for any endpoint. `grep -n "429\|407" server.py` also confirms the backend itself never emits either code. Whatever Jono is seeing as 429/407 on-device is not coming from this app or Railway's edge.
- **Confirmed external cause, independently verified, not a guess**: Supabase's own public status page (`status.supabase.com`) shows an **ongoing, unresolved incident** - "401 errors due to JWT rejections," component **API Gateway**, status `identified`, impact `minor`, started 2026-08-14, last updated 2026-09-02 - still live. The components endpoint confirms API Gateway's *current* status right now is `degraded_performance`. Every single Supabase call this backend makes (every `/students`, `/classrooms`, `/creatures`, etc. endpoint) goes through exactly this component. This lines up with the timing (widespread multi-second latency observed live, at the exact time of the report) and is a real, externally-confirmed, pre-existing condition - not caused by anything in today's session (no deploy happened today; the PT Storage renames went directly through Supabase's Storage API, a separate subsystem from the API Gateway/PostgREST layer this incident affects).

**2. Today's role-gating changes (A90) - no retry/loop found in what I added; a real, pre-existing redundant-fetch pattern found instead, unrelated to today's work.**
- `refreshStudents()`/`refreshClassrooms()` (the functions this session's A90 fix touched) each do exactly one `await`, no retry-on-failure logic of any kind, and swallow errors quietly - confirmed by re-reading the current code. Today's change added an early role check before the network call; it didn't add any looping, retry, or re-fire behaviour.
- Found instead, in code that predates this session: `teacher/dashboard.tsx`'s `useFocusEffect` calls `loadData()` + `refreshStudents()` + `refreshClassrooms()` together on **every screen-focus event** (i.e. every time a user navigates back to this screen, not just on first mount) - and `loadData()` **itself independently fetches `/api/classrooms` a second time** as its own "fallback" (line ~200), duplicating `refreshClassrooms()`'s call on the very same focus event. Confirmed the effect's own dependency array (`[loadData, refreshAlertCount]`, both properly memoized with stable `useCallback` deps) does NOT create an infinite render loop - it only re-fires on genuine navigation focus, not every render. But real back-and-forth navigation during a long device-pass session (exactly what today looked like) means `/classrooms` gets hit 2x per return-to-dashboard from multiple call sites, on top of `student/select.tsx` and `teacher/classrooms.tsx` each independently calling the same two refreshers on their own mount. This is very likely why `/students`/`/classrooms` specifically are the ones that time out while creature-batch endpoints (called once per relevant screen, not duplicated) mostly stay under the timeout even though they're individually just as slow - not a new bug, but a real amplifier landing on top of Supabase's confirmed degraded API Gateway.

**3. Client timeout - 30 seconds, confirmed in code; the observed 2-8s responses are genuinely slow, not blocked.**
- `frontend/src/utils/api.ts`: `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 30000`, enforced via `AbortController` (added Aug 26, item 8 - this networking layer had zero timeout handling before that). The response times actually observed in Railway's logs (2-8s) are well under 30s and mostly succeeded (200) - real but moderate slowness, not an outright block. The two confirmed 499s suggest at least some requests ran meaningfully longer than the 2-8s sample caught, long enough to trip the 30s abort, consistent with Supabase's degraded gateway causing occasional much-longer tail latency beyond what a small log sample shows.

**Not investigated / can't verify from here**: Supabase's actual DB connection-pool saturation (would need the Supabase dashboard's own Database/pooling stats or a direct `psql` connection - only the REST API is available in this environment) - worth a look if the API Gateway incident doesn't fully explain the pattern once Jono checks it live. The literal 429/407 codes Jono is seeing on-device remain unexplained by anything server-side; a school/corporate network proxy on the test device's network (407 = Proxy Authentication Required is a classic captive-proxy signature, not something a cloud host would normally inject) is the most likely explanation given neither Railway nor this backend ever produces either code - worth checking whether the timeouts/errors happen on the same network every time, or follow the device across networks.

No code changed. Nothing committed.

**Accepted by Jono, not blocking this PR - logged for follow-up:**
1. **Build 27**: fix the redundant-fetch pattern found in #2 above - `teacher/dashboard.tsx`'s `useFocusEffect` fires `loadData` + `refreshStudents` + `refreshClassrooms` on every focus, and `loadData` itself double-fetches `/api/classrooms` on top of that. Consolidate to one fetch path with basic staleness (e.g. skip refetch if the cached data is <30s old). Also softens exposure to Supabase's own tail latency, whatever its cause.
2. **Monitor**: Supabase's ongoing API Gateway degraded-performance incident (since 2026-08-14, still `identified`/unresolved as of this check) is the likely source of the slow `/students`/`/classrooms` responses. Revisit if the slowness persists after Supabase's own status page shows the incident resolved.

## A97 — IT red_4/red_5: genuine duplicate-file bug (not a text bug), plus a structural pass across ES/DE/HI/ZH/RU, 2026-09-06

**red_6, confirmed non-issue** (Jono's own call, no investigation needed): "Abbracciati" (text) / "datti un abbraccio" (audio) are the same self-hug concept, just phrased differently - left as-is.

**red_4, diagnosed - genuine audio bug, NOT a translation bug. Nothing renamed, per instruction.**
- (a) Canonical concept for `red_4` across every language checked: **Safe Space** - EN `DEFAULT_HELPERS`: "Safe Space"; PT: "Espaço Seguro"; `HELPERS_IT` itself: "Spazio Sicuro" (= Safe Space). The IT **text** is already correct and in the right position - Jono's hypothesis ("if the concept is Ask-for-Help everywhere else, the IT string is wrong") doesn't hold; the concept is Safe Space everywhere, IT included. Do not touch `it.json`/`HELPERS_IT` text.
- Re-read `HELPERS_IT`'s entire red array end to end: `red_1` Congelare (Freeze), `red_2` Respiri Profondi (Big Breaths), `red_3` Contare al Contrario (Count Backwards), `red_4` Spazio Sicuro (Safe Space), `red_5` Chiedi Aiuto (Ask for Help), `red_6` Abbraccio a Sé Stessi (Self Hug) - **all 6 in exactly the canonical order**, no other IT red slot is misplaced. `red_4` is the only anomaly in this colour.
- (b) **Root cause found, not guessed**: queried the real files directly. `it/red_4.m4a` and `it/red_5.m4a` are **byte-for-byte identical** - confirmed via matching Supabase ETags (`6f17e2229679d2328c11b45020f68d78` for both) and identical size (19648 bytes each). This is why `red_4`'s card plays "Chiedi Aiuto" - its audio file **is** a copy of `red_5`'s file, not a mislabeled-but-distinct recording. Checked the full bucket listing from A95's audit for a stray/orphaned "Spazio Sicuro" file hiding under a different name anywhere else in Storage - none exists; all 28 IT files are accounted for under their expected canonical names, so there is no third file to rename in from elsewhere.
- **Consequence**: this can't be fixed by renaming, unlike the PT case - there is currently no genuine "Spazio Sicuro" recording anywhere in Storage for Italian. This needs a **new recording** of red_4 (Spazio Sicuro), not a file move. Reported before touching anything, per instruction.

**Structural plausibility pass, ES/DE/HI/ZH/RU** - checked every language against the two concrete fingerprints the PT and IT bugs actually left behind, not a vague guess:
- **Text-order check** (same method as the red_4 diagnosis above): pulled every `HELPERS_<LANG>` array's full id->name order and compared against canonical `DEFAULT_HELPERS`. All 5 languages match canonical order **exactly**, all 24 helpers, zero exceptions.
- **Duplicate-file check** (the fingerprint that caught IT's red_4/red_5): compared file sizes within each language's 28 files for any same-size collision. **None found** in ES, DE, HI, ZH, or RU - IT's red_4/red_5 remains the only such pair anywhere in the bucket.
- **Upload-batch check** (the fingerprint that caught PT's blue_2/blue_4 rotation): pulled real `created_at` timestamps for all 24 helper files per language. ES, DE, HI, ZH, and RU each show one single, smooth, continuous upload sequence with no gaps or out-of-order clusters - unlike PT (which had a visible two-batch signature: blue_2/blue_4 uploaded distinctly later than blue_1/3/5/6) and unlike IT's own separately-documented Aug 30 manual-reorder history. This is a real structural difference, not just an absence of evidence.
- **Recommendation**: none of ES/DE/HI/ZH/RU show either fingerprint that actually caught a real bug elsewhere - the audit is clean, not merely untested. Given Jono can personally verify ES/DE by ear, a quick spot-listen there is still worthwhile as final confirmation but low-priority given the clean structural result. HI/ZH/RU can't be verified by him directly, and are already flagged in this codebase's own `settings.tsx` comment as `isBeta`/"unreviewed beyond the voiced strings themselves" - since no check I can run substitutes for an actual native speaker confirming recorded content (only text-position and file-duplication bugs are catchable this way), recommend a native-speaker review pass for HI/ZH/RU specifically as a build 27/ongoing-quality item, not because anything concrete was found wrong.

No code changed, no files renamed. Nothing committed.

**Agreed and logged, Jono's calls:**

No app change needed once the real recording exists (the file naming/lookup convention already does the rest).

## A98 — fix/pdf-report-pagination: student PDF LayoutError fixed, branched/tested/pushed, 2026-09-06

PR #3 merged and confirmed healthy on Railway (new deploy `bd11182e`, SUCCESS, 0% 5xx, clean boot logs, live endpoint verified responding correctly) before starting this. Branched `fix/pdf-report-pagination` off `origin/main` per instruction, not off the working `main` (which still carries its own 3 unpushed kiosk/audio commits, left untouched pending Jono's reconcile decision).

**Root cause, re-confirmed in the actual code** (originally diagnosed in A85 via live production reproduction): `generate_pdf_report`'s check-in log table (`log_tbl`) had `splitByRow=0` (forbids the table from ever breaking across a page) AND was wrapped in `KeepTogether([log_heading, Spacer(1,4), log_tbl])` (demands the WHOLE thing fit on one page) - two settings actively fighting the one thing that would have saved it: `repeatRows=1`, already present, exists specifically so a table can safely split across pages while repeating its header. For a busy month (49 rows) the table's real height (816pt) exceeds the usable page height (757pt) - no setting combination here could satisfy both constraints, so ReportLab raised a hard `LayoutError` and the whole endpoint 500'd.

**Fix**: `splitByRow=1` (ReportLab's actual default - set explicitly since it's the exact property that was disabled) lets the table break across pages. `KeepTogether` now wraps only `[log_heading, Spacer(1,4)]` - always tiny, always fits, so the heading is never orphaned alone at a page bottom - not the table, which now flows and splits freely right after with its own `repeatRows=1` repeating the header row on every continuation page. Font size (`ST_SMALL`, 7.5pt) untouched, per instruction - real pagination replaces any need to shrink text to compensate.

**Tested, not just asserted**: a standalone reportlab script mirroring the real function's exact table setup (same colWidths, styles, colours, margins), built with 65 mocked rows (busier than the real 49-row case that crashed) - confirmed the OLD settings (`splitByRow=0` + table-wrapping `KeepTogether`) reproduce the identical `LayoutError` on this mocked data too, confirming the test genuinely exercises the real bug rather than passing by construction. The NEW settings build a clean 3-page PDF from the same 65 rows. `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(...)"` confirms the edited file is syntactically valid.

Committed, pushed, PR opened: **https://github.com/classofhappiness/Class-of-Happiness/pull/4**. Not merged - merging it auto-deploys; Jono will verify by generating Matilda's real August 2026 PDF against production afterward.
